# Pi sweep review — CastCircle

Exhaustive per-file pass: 202 code files across 110 batches — model ds4/GLM-5.3-Flash-Q2:off — 2026-08-30.

## Findings

- [medium] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:130-142 — migration step failure handler swallows only benign "already applied" errors but the benign-detection is string-matching on the exception message; a driver that words the duplicate error differently (or a localized message) makes a real failure rethrow correctly, but a *benign* one that doesn't match the strings is logged and rethrown, aborting the whole upgrade chain — direction of failure is toward the riskier side: a partially-applied migration leaves user_version unadvanced and every subsequent launch re-runs the same failing step forever — smallest safe fix: match on the exception type/SQLSTATE (e.g. `SqlException` with duplicate-column code) instead of substring matching, or at minimum advance user_version per completed step so a rethrow doesn't wedge the upgrade permanently.
- [medium] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:146-188 — onUpgrade runs steps sequentially with no transaction wrapper; a crash mid-upgrade leaves some columns/indexes applied and user_version not advanced, so the next launch re-runs earlier steps — the `_step` "already applied" tolerance covers duplicate-column/index, but any *other* partial state (e.g. index created but column add failed) wedges the upgrade — smallest safe fix: wrap the whole onUpgrade body in `transaction()` so each version bump is atomic, or advance user_version after each successful step.
- [low] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:316-334 — `_openConnection` executes PRAGMAs in `setup` on the connection thread before the background executor; `journal_mode=WAL` on a fresh LazyDatabase is fine, but if the file lives on a network mount (not typical for app docs dir) WAL fails silently and the pragma result is ignored — smallest safe fix: check the returned result of `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` and fall back to DELETE journal mode if it isn't 'wal'. (Reachability: operator/local device only.)
- [info] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:328-330 — verify PRAGMA statements are not user-visible log output; no secret exposure found in this file.
- [low] lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart:10-13 — `CastRole.fromString` degrades unknown role strings to `CastRole.primary` silently; a malformed/newer cloud row claiming an organizer role becomes "primary" — direction of failure is permissive: an unknown *higher-privilege* role string is downgraded, but an unknown *lower-privilege* string is also upgraded to primary, granting a member more cast privileges than the source row intended — smallest safe fix: log the unknown value and map unknown to the least-privileged role (`understudy`) instead of `primary`.
- [low] lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart:46 — `hasJoined` derives join state solely from `userId != null`; a member row with a userId but null `joinedAt` (or vice versa) yields inconsistent joined/invited state with no validation — smallest safe fix: treat `joinedAt != null` as the join signal, or assert consistency on load.

## Coverage
lib/data/database/app_database.dart — findings: 4
lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart — findings: 2
- [info] lib/data/models/production_models.dart:19 — `locale` silently defaults to 'en-US' when omitted — a caller constructing a Production without a locale gets an English-US STT locale rather than failing or inheriting the device locale, so rehearsal transcription can run in the wrong language with no error — make the constructor require `locale` (or default to null and resolve at use site from the device locale) — reachability: any authenticated user creating a production via a code path that omits the locale; consequence is silent wrong-language behavior, not data loss, so capped at info/low.
- [info] lib/data/models/rehearsal_models.dart:38 — `struggledLines` filters on `bestScore < 0.7` with a hardcoded threshold — the "below threshold" semantics are baked into the model, so any caller wanting a different threshold (or a threshold supplied by the production config) cannot express it, and the constant is invisible to tests that assert against it — expose the threshold as a parameter or move the filter to the caller that owns the policy — test-only/analytics helper, low.
- [info] lib/data/models/rehearsal_models.dart:33-34 — `completionRate` returns 0.0 when `totalLines` is 0 — a session with zero lines reports "0% complete" indistinguishably from a session that failed to record totals; direction of failure resolves to the misleading-but-safe value — consider returning null or a sentinel for the no-data case — analytics-only, low.
- [info] lib/data/models/production_models.dart:23-42 — `copyWith` accepts nullable params for every field including `id`/`createdAt`, so a caller can silently replace the identity/timestamp of a Production — no validation guards identity fields; if any caller passes `id:` from user input this becomes a mass-assignment-style overwrite of the record key — no caller is visible in this batch, so this is an assumption; restrict copyWith to mutable fields or assert identity fields are unchanged — low.

## Coverage
lib/data/models/production_models.dart — findings: 2
lib/data/models/rehearsal_models.dart — findings: 2
- [medium] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:243-246 — markRecordingUploaded persists a caller-supplied remoteUrl with no validation (no scheme/host allowlist, no length cap) — a malicious or malformed URL string (e.g. `javascript:`, `file:`, or an attacker-controlled host) is stored verbatim and later handed to playback/launch code that treats it as a web URL, enabling open-redirect-style navigation or playback failure on every cast device that loads the recording — validate the URL (allow http/https, reject `javascript:`/`file:`/`data:` schemes, cap length) before delegating to `_db.markRecordingUploaded`, or normalize it at the model boundary.
- [medium] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:29-40 — saveProduction writes a full row via `insertProduction` with no upsert/ownership check; if the caller passes an existing `production.id` the insert either silently fails (Drift default insert mode) or, when the DB layer uses `InsertMode.insertOrReplace`, overwrites another user's row — because the repository never verifies that the caller owns `production.id`, an authenticated user can clobber a production they don't own (IDOR/mass-assignment) — switch to an explicit upsert guarded by a `WHERE id = ? AND organizerId = caller` check, or reject saves whose `id` already exists and belongs to a different organizer.
- [medium] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:84-95 — saveCastMember has the same unguarded insert shape: `insertCastMember` with `Value(member.productionId)` and no check that `member.productionId` refers to a production the caller controls, so a forged `productionId` lets a user inject cast rows into another tenant's production (cross-tenant write) — enforce that `member.productionId` matches a production owned by the current user before delegating to the DB layer.
- [medium] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:224-236 — saveRecording likewise inserts a `RecordingsCompanion` with caller-chosen `productionId`/`scriptLineId` and no tenant/ownership validation, allowing recordings to be attached to productions the user does not own (IDOR write) — validate `productionId` ownership (and that `scriptLineId` belongs to that production) before insert.
- [medium] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:42-61 — deleteProduction performs best-effort file deletion but swallows all exceptions (`catch (_)`) with no logging, so a failed file unlink (permission error, path in use) is silently ignored and the recording file is orphaned on disk while the DB row is removed — at minimum log the swallowed error (debugPrint or the app's log service) so operators can detect accumulating orphaned files; the DB-row deletion path itself is fine.
- [low] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:46-49 — `File(recording.localPath)` is constructed from a DB-stored path with no containment check; if `localPath` was ever populated from an untrusted source (e.g. a shared/imported script bundle), deletion could target an arbitrary filesystem path (path-traversal-style unlink) — clamp deletion to the app's documents/recordings directory (verify `localPath` starts with the expected base dir) before `file.delete()`.
- [low] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:137 — `multiCharacters.join(',')` round-trips through `_scriptLineFromRow`'s `split(',')` (lines 162-164); a character name containing a comma (e.g. "JANE, SMITH") is silently split into two characters on read, corrupting the multi-character list — switch the persisted separator to a character that cannot appear in names (e.g. `\u0000` or JSON-encode the list) or escape commas on write and unescape on read.
- [low] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:189 — same comma-as-separator defect for `characters.join(',')` in `saveScenes`, read back via `split(',')` at lines 206-208 — scene character lists with commas in names are silently corrupted — apply the same escaping/alternative-separator fix as the script-lines case.
- [low] lib/data/models/voice_preset.dart:147-149 — `VoicePresets.byId` silently falls back to `modernAmerican` for any unknown id, so a typo'd or renamed preset id produces the wrong production style with no error surfaced to the caller — return `null` (or throw) for unknown ids so callers can surface the mismatch instead of silently picking a default preset.
- [info] lib/data/models/voice_preset.dart:60-131 — preset voice lists reference only a subset of the IDs declared in `voiceLabels` (e.g. `af_alloy`, `af_aoede`, `af_kore`, `af_nicole`, `af_river`, `af_sky`, `am_echo`, `am_fenrir`, `am_liam`, `am_puck` are labeled but never used in any preset) — verify these unused labels are intentional (e.g. reserved for future presets) rather than a sign that a preset was meant to include them; no action needed if confirmed.

## Coverage
lib/data/models/voice_preset.dart — findings: 2
lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart — findings: 8
- [medium] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:60-63 — cache eviction removes the oldest entry before inserting the new one, but the check runs on every call including cache hits' siblings, so a burst of ≥512 distinct paths evicts entries that may still be in use; more importantly the eviction happens even when the incoming `volume` was computed from a failed/exception path (volume=1.0), so a transient channel failure permanently caches 1.0 for that path — consequence: a recording whose loudness analysis failed once (e.g. plugin not registered on Android, decode error) plays at full volume forever until `invalidate`/`clear` is called, silently defeating the loudness-normalization feature with no error surfaced; smallest safe fix: only cache the computed gain on success (move the `_gainCache[path] = volume` write inside the try-success branch, or track a success flag and skip caching when analysis threw/unavailable), and keep the 512-cap eviction only on successful inserts.
- [low] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:68-70 — `prefetch` fires `volumeFor(path)` without awaiting or guarding, so concurrent `volumeFor` calls for the same uncached path each invoke the native channel (duplicate work) and the fire-and-forget future's errors are already swallowed inside, but if `volumeFor` is ever changed to throw before the try block the unawaited future becomes an unhandled async error; consequence: wasted channel round-trips and a latent unhandled-error path; smallest safe fix: `unawaited(volumeFor(path))` with an explicit comment, or guard with an in-flight future map.
- [info] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:36-55 — `volumeFor`'s catch-all `catch (_)` swallows every error class including programming errors (e.g. a `TypeError` from a malformed channel result) and maps them to volume 1.0; consequence: misconfiguration of the method-channel contract is silently masked as "analysis unavailable"; smallest safe fix: narrow the catch to `PlatformException`/`MissingPluginException` and let unexpected types propagate (or log via debugPrint).
- [info] lib/data/services/analytics_service.dart:10-11 — `_analytics` is gated on `firebaseAvailable` imported from `../../main.dart`; the guard's correctness depends on that flag being initialized before any log call — if events fire before main.dart sets it, events silently drop (or, if the flag defaults true before availability is known, calls hit `FirebaseAnalytics.instance` pre-init); consequence: analytics events lost or crash depending on flag default; smallest safe fix: verify `firebaseAvailable` is a lazily-initialized, default-safe value in main.dart (assumption: not visible in this batch).

- [medium] lib/data/services/demo_production_service.dart:59-93 — `load()` mutates shared provider state and persists demo data without any guard against concurrent/re-entrant invocation; a second caller racing the first (e.g., double-tap on the demo entry point before `existing` resolves) can pass the `existing == null` check twice and add duplicate demo productions — duplicate rows in the productions list and a stale `currentProductionProvider` — smallest safe fix: re-check `existing` after the async gaps (or make the add idempotent by keying on `productionId` inside the notifier) before calling `add`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/demo_production_service.dart:84 — `persistScriptLocally` is awaited with no error handling; a local storage failure (disk full, permission) throws out of `load()` after `currentScriptProvider` was already set, leaving the app in a half-initialized demo state with no rollback — smallest safe fix: wrap the persistence call in try/catch and either surface a user-facing error or revert the provider writes on failure.
- [low] lib/data/services/demo_production_service.dart:99-113 — `_preselectCharacter` writes the chosen character to `SharedPreferences` under a fixed key `rehearsal_character_$productionId` but never validates the stored value against `script.characters`; a stale/corrupt pref (e.g., after a script re-cut removes that character) sets `rehearsalCharacterProvider` to a name with no lines, silently breaking rehearsal playback for that user — smallest safe fix: verify the stored character exists in `script.characters` (or re-run the fallback selection) before assigning the provider state.
- [low] lib/data/services/espeak_heteronyms.dart:85-88 — `before.lastIndexOf(word)` can return `-1` when the matched group text differs in case from what `lastIndexOf` searches for (the regex is case-insensitive but `lastIndexOf` is case-sensitive), so `replaceRange(-1, -1 + word.length, ...)` throws a `RangeError` or replaces the wrong span — e.g., input "LIVE and let live" where group 2 matched "LIVE" but `before` contains "LIVE" only if the full match preserved it; when the prefix group matched lowercase "long " and group 2 matched "LIVE", `before.lastIndexOf("LIVE")` works, but with mixed case like "liVe" the case-sensitive search misses — smallest safe fix: compute the replacement offset from `m.start(group)`/`m.group(group)` spans instead of a case-sensitive `lastIndexOf` on the matched text.
- [info] lib/data/services/espeak_heteronyms.dart:66-70 — `_matchCase` treats an all-lowercase original as "no change" and an initial-cap original via the `original[0]` branch, but a fully-uppercase multi-char replacement path is only handled by the first branch; for a single-character uppercase original like "L" the first branch fires correctly, yet for mixed-case originals (e.g., "liVe") neither branch applies and capitalisation is silently dropped — verify intended behavior for mixed-case inputs; smallest safe fix: document or handle the mixed-case case explicitly.

- [medium] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:45-46 — percentile index can go out of range when the list is empty or tiny — `_pct` indexes `sorted[((sorted.length - 1) * p).round()]`; with an empty list the index is -1 (or with length 1 and p=0.99 the index rounds to 0, fine, but empty → -1) and `_report` is only guarded by `_buildMs.isNotEmpty` for the build list, not for `_rasterMs` — a frame with build timing but no raster timing (or a timing list where raster entries were never added) makes `_pct(r, 0.9)` throw RangeError inside the log callback — guard `_pct` with `if (sorted.isEmpty) return 0;` and clamp the index.
- [low] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:37 — jank denominator uses frame count `n` from `_buildMs.length` while `_jank` counts frames whose `totalSpan` exceeded 16.7ms, but `totalSpan` is read without a null/zero guard and frames with zero-length spans (skipped/coalesced frames) inflate the jank percentage — consequence is only a misreported metric in field logs, so low; consider counting jank only for frames with a non-zero build/raster duration.
- [info] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:24-29 — `install()` sets `_installed = true` before `SchedulerBinding.instance.addTimingsCallback` is reached; if `addTimingsCallback` throws (e.g. called before `WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized`), the service is permanently marked installed and can never be re-installed — set `_installed = true` only after the callback registration succeeds.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:203-211 — cache-hit path returns the cached path without checking that the isolate/engine is usable, but more importantly the LRU touch at 208-209 is fire-and-forget with `catchError((_) {})`, so a failed mtime update silently degrades pruning order to FIFO — acceptable as designed, but the real defect: `synthesize` returns `cachePath` even when `_toIsolate == null && _starting == null` is false only for the cache-miss branch; on a cache miss with the engine never started and `_starting == null` but `_toIsolate == null`, line 213-215 returns `null` — correct — however when `_starting` is non-null but the prior start failed (completes `false`), the new request is queued and `_pump()` sends nothing (port null), and `req.completer.future` never completes because `onDone` only fires if the isolate died after being listened to; a failed `_start` that returned `false` before spawning leaves queued requests hanging forever — after a failed start, drain `_queue` completers with `null` (same shape as the `onDone` handler at 149-167).
- [high] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:213-215 — if `ensureStarted` was never called (or the model isn't downloaded), `synthesize` silently returns `null` for the first call and, worse, when `_starting != null` but that future resolves `false` (model missing), every subsequent `synthesize` enqueues a request whose completer is never completed (no isolate, no `onDone`), leaking the request and leaving the caller awaiting forever — check the resolved start result: if `_start` completed `false`, fail queued/in-flight requests immediately instead of parking them.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:228-231 — urgent cancellation completes dropped queued requests with `null` but never aborts the currently-generating request unless `_cancelBelow.value` is set — that IS set at 232, fine — but the `removeWhere` predicate completes the completer and then returns `q.urgent`; a non-urgent request whose completer was already completed elsewhere (e.g. by `onDone` failure drain) would be double-completed — `Completer.complete` on an already-completed completer throws `StateError`, and the `removeWhere` body has no try/catch, so one already-completed entry makes the whole urgent path throw and skip inserting the new urgent request — guard with `if (!q.completer.isCompleted)` before completing (the same unguarded pattern appears at 344-348 in `stop()` and 158-163 in the `onDone` handler, where `stop()` completes `_inFlight` without an `isCompleted` check).
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:344 — `stop()` completes `_inFlight.completer` unconditionally; if the request was already completed (e.g. the isolate replied with a path just before `stop`), this throws `StateError: Future already completed` from a `Future<void> stop()` — the exception propagates to the caller of `stop()` (including the failure path at 176 `await stop()`), aborting teardown so `_sub`/`_isolate` are never nulled and the isolate leaks — wrap completions in `if (!c.isCompleted)` guards (same root cause as the queued-loop completions at 346-348 and the drain at 158-163).
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:127-131 — `ready` completer is completed without an `isCompleted` guard on the `ready:true` branch only via `!ready.isCompleted` (present), but the `initError` branch at 129-131 completes `ready` with `false` guarded — however if the isolate sends `ready:true` twice (e.g. after a re-init), the second send is ignored silently rather than logged; low-value, skip — the actual defect here: `msg['path'] as String?` at 143 and `msg['seq']` at 135 use unchecked casts inside the listen callback; a malformed message from the isolate (or a hostile/tampered payload if any code sends arbitrary maps) throws a `CastError` inside the port listener, killing the subscription silently and leaving requests pending — use `as String?`/`as int?` with null checks or `tryParse` and log unexpected message shapes.
- [low] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:431 — `cancelBelow.value` is read from a shared `Pointer<Int32>` written on the main isolate (232) and read in the isolate callback (431, 448); FFI shared memory across isolates is fine here because the address was passed, but the write at 232 is not followed by any memory barrier guarantee documented in the code — in practice dart:ffi shared pointers across isolates on the same process are coherent, so this is informational only; no fix needed beyond a comment.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:471-472 — `_writeWav` writes to `tmpDir/kokoro_onnx_<fileSeq>.wav` with a monotonically increasing per-isolate counter, but the file is never deleted after adoption into the cache (rename at 247) or after a failed adoption; every synthesized WAV (≈48 KB/s of audio) accumulates in the temporary directory for the process lifetime, and the 150 MB LRU prune at 278-318 only walks `kokoro_cache`, not the tmp WAVs — disk grows unboundedly across a long rehearsal session — delete the tmp WAV after a successful `renameSync` (rename moves it, so only delete on the `catch`/null-path branches) and on aborted/empty generations.
- [low] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:502-504 — sample clamping writes `v < -32768 ? -32768 : (v > 32767 ? 32767 : v)` but `setInt16` on a value of exactly 32768 after rounding of 1.0*32767 = 32767 is fine; however `samples[i]` outside [-1, 1] beyond ±2 would still clamp — correct — the real nit: `(samples[i] * 32767).round()` on NaN produces NaN and `setInt16` throws; if sherpa ever returns NaN samples the whole generation errors — guard with a finite check. - [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:111-121 — `_start()` awaits `ready.future` with a 30s timeout that resolves to `false`, then calls `stop()` on timeout; but `stop()` increments `_epoch` and kills the isolate while `ensureStarted`'s `whenComplete` may still be registering — a timeout path can tear down a fresh start that a concurrent caller already replaced, leaving `ensureStarted` returning `false` for a start that actually succeeded — callers that treat `false` as "unavailable" permanently skip live matching — make the timeout path return `false` without invoking the full `stop()` teardown (or only dispose the in-flight epoch's resources).
- [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:139-148 — `stop()` sends `{'cmd': 'dispose'}` then immediately kills the isolate with `Isolate.beforeNextEvent`; the isolate's `dispose` handler (line 237-241) closes the commands port and frees native memory, but if the kill lands before the isolate processes the message, `recognizer.free()`/`stream.free()` never run and native memory leaks per start/stop cycle — drain the dispose ack (or kill with `Isolate.immediate` after a short delay) before killing.
- [low] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:227 — `uid = msg['uid'] as int? ?? uid + 1;` — the isolate-side `uid` is overwritten from the start command, but `decodeAndReport` (line 206) always sends with the isolate's current `uid`; if the main-side `startUtterance` sends a uid that skips ahead, subsequent partials from the old utterance are tagged with the new uid and are not dropped by the main-side stale filter (line 104) — keep the isolate-side uid monotonic per utterance and only advance it on `start`.
- [low] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:135 — `endUtterance` sends `{'cmd': 'end'}` but the isolate's `end` case (line 229-235) feeds 12800 samples of silence unconditionally; if `endUtterance` is called without a preceding `startUtterance` after a `dispose`/restart, `_toIsolate` may be a stale port from a previous epoch — guard `endUtterance`/`feedPcm` on the same epoch check used by `startUtterance` (they already no-op via `?.` if `_toIsolate` is null, but a stale non-null port from a prior epoch is not nulled by `stop()`'s `_toIsolate = null` only if stop ran — verify callers cannot invoke on a half-torn-down instance).
- [low] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:78-94 — `_handleNativeCall` casts `call.arguments as String` without null/type check; a native plugin sending a Map or null arguments throws a cast exception inside the method-call handler, silently dropping the remote command — use `call.arguments as String?` and guard, or pattern-match with `if (call.arguments is String)`.
- [low] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:82-91 — `case 'jumpBack': case 'playPause':` fall-through routes both `jumpBack` and `playPause` to `onPlayPause?.call()`; the documented mapping (lines 8-10) says previous-track/jump-back should invoke `onJumpBack`, so a jump-back command never reaches the jump-back handler — add `onJumpBack?.call(); break;` for the `jumpBack` case (the comment at 84-88 claims this is intentional future-proofing, but the wired handler `onJumpBack` at line 17 is then dead code — verify against the iOS plugin contract before "fixing").

- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:242-244 — native method-call handler casts `call.arguments` to `Map` and reads `modelId`/`progress`/`size`/`error` without validating types or null — a malformed or hostile platform message (e.g. a compromised plugin or a spoofed channel call) throws inside the handler, leaving `_states[modelId]` un-updated or a stale `downloading` state stuck forever, and the uncaught exception can crash the app — validate `args is Map<String, Object?>` and null-check each field before use (same shape at 253-255, 294-296).
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:264-266 — `onDownloadComplete` resolves the model with `availableModels.where((m) => m.id == modelId).firstOrNull` but never checks `model == null` before calling `_verifyDownload(model)` — a completion event for an unknown/retired modelId dereferences a null `AiModel` and throws, so the download state never reaches `downloaded`/`error` and the UI shows a perpetual spinner — guard `if (model == null) { mark error state; break; }` before verification.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:315-334 — `fileProblem` treats a file whose size is *larger* than expected as valid when `exactSizeBytes` is null (only the truncation floor `actual < sizeBytes ~/ 2` is checked) — a corrupted/overwritten file (e.g. an HTML error page saved over the model path by a prior bug) passes the readiness check and gets fed to TTS/ASR engines as if installed — also compare `actual > model.sizeBytes` (or an upper bound) for mutable upstreams, or require `sha256` verification whenever the size check alone is inconclusive.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:331-333 — truncation floor uses `~/ 2` (integer division) so a file exactly half the expected size (e.g. 81,794,259 B for a 163,588,519 B model) is accepted as "usable" — a download truncated at exactly 50% silently passes and the recognizer/TTS loads a corrupt model — use `actual <= model.sizeBytes ~/ 2` or compare against a stricter floor (e.g. `sizeBytes - 1`).
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:406-413 — `_verifyDownload` computes SHA-256 by streaming the file, but on `catch (e)` it sets `problem = 'sha256 could not be computed: $e'` and then, at 416-423, deletes the file only `if (problem != null && file.existsSync())` — a transient I/O error (file locked, read failure) marks the model errored AND deletes a possibly-good file, forcing a full re-download of a 164 MB model — distinguish "hash mismatch" (delete) from "hash unreadable" (keep, retry later).
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:596-616 — the redirect loop in `_dartDownload` follows redirects manually but never re-checks `res.statusCode` after the final hop when `isRedirect` is false yet status is 3xx-without-Location, and `redirectsLeft` is decremented *before* the throw check at 612 so a 6th redirect throws with `redirectsLeft == -1` while the message claims "too many" — minor, but the loop also never drains the response body of intermediate 3xx responses that lack a `location` header, leaking sockets — drain the body on every non-200 hop and decrement after the bounds check.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:623-625 — `total` falls back to `model.sizeBytes` when `res.contentLength <= 0` or the response is gzipped, but the progress clamp `(received / total).clamp(0.0, 0.99)` at 639 then reports progress against a *declared* size that may not match the wire bytes — if the server sends more bytes than `sizeBytes` (e.g. a CDN serving a different revision), progress pins at 0.99 and the UI never reaches 1.0 before completion — clamp against `max(total, received)` or track `received` alone when `total` is unknown.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:645-651 — when the response arrives gzipped despite the `identity` request, the code pipes it through `gzip.decoder` but the `received` byte counter (incremented at 633 on the *pre-decompression* stream) still drives progress and the `total` comparison — harmless for correctness of the file, but the reported progress can exceed 1.0 relative to decoded bytes — count decoded bytes for progress, or document the wire-byte basis.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:652-658 — `await for (final chunk in body) { sink.add(chunk); }` never drains or closes the original `res` stream when gzip decoding is active — the raw response stream's subscription is dropped once `gzip.decoder.bind` ends, leaving the underlying socket open until the client is closed at 689 — explicitly `await res.drain<void>()` or `res.close()` after the decoded stream completes.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:677-687 — the outer `catch (e)` in `_dartDownload` swallows *all* exception types (including `StateError`, `ArgumentError` from logic bugs) and converts them into a user-visible "download failed" state, hiding programming errors during development — catch only `IOException`/`HttpException`/`WebSocketException` and rethrow unexpected types.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:795-832 — `_freeDiskSpaceBytes` calls `statvfs` via FFI with hand-rolled struct offsets (820-822) and no `Isolate` guard; if the Darwin layout assumption is wrong on a future OS release, the sanity check `bavail > blocks` returns null (safe) but `frsize <= 0` etc. can still pass on garbage and return a wildly wrong free-space number, causing the preflight at 517 to wrongly *allow* a download that fills the disk — add a second sanity bound (e.g. `bavail * frsize` must be < some plausible max, or cross-check `f_bfree`) before trusting the value.
- [low] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:107-214 — `availableModels` pins SHA-256 hashes and exact sizes as compile-time constants sourced from external release assets; if any upstream asset is ever re-published under the same tag with different bytes, every user's download fails verification with no recovery path except a new app release — acceptable by design (documented at 155-157), but consider a remote "known-good manifest" fallback channel for emergency rotation.

- [low] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:778-783 — FFI typedefs declare `_StatvfsNative`/`_MallocNative` with `Pointer<Uint8>`/`IntPtr` parameter types that must match the C ABI exactly; a mismatch would be silent UB rather than a thrown error — verify against the Darwin `statvfs`/`malloc`/`free` signatures in a unit test with a known-good volume.
- [info] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:122-123,143-144,165-166,179-180,193-194,207-208 — pinned SHA-256 hex strings are embedded as source constants; they are public asset hashes, not secrets, but confirm none of the surrounding release-tooling files (not in this batch) embed signing keys or tokens alongside them.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:55-62 — `isKokoroReady` checks only file *existence* (`File(...).exists()`), never size or hash, so a truncated `model.fp16.onnx` (e.g. from a crashed extraction) reports "ready" and `getKokoroPaths` hands a corrupt model to the ONNX runtime — reuse `ModelDownloadService.fileProblem`-style size/hash verification, or at minimum compare `File.lengthSync()` against the archive's declared per-file sizes.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:104-122 — `downloadKokoro` calls `_reclaimLegacyKokoro()` (which deletes the old fp32 pack) *before* checking whether the new fp16 download will succeed; if the archive download or extraction then fails, the user has lost the working legacy model with no fallback — reclaim only after the new archive has been verified and extracted successfully.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:185-186 — `if (await File(archivePath).exists()) await File(archivePath).delete();` inside `try { } catch (_) {}` swallows deletion failures silently, so a locked/partial archive from a previous crash can persist and be *reused* as if it were a fresh download (the size check at 198 then passes on stale bytes) — log the failure or delete to a unique temp name per attempt.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:196-199 — the archive sanity check is `archiveSize < 1000` — a 1 KB+ truncated/tampered archive passes this gate and only fails later at the SHA-256 step (205-215); when `expectedSha256` is null (callers may pass none), a corrupt archive is extracted and fed to native inference with no integrity gate at all — require a non-null `expectedSha256` for this call site, or add a minimum-size floor derived from the known archive (~180 MB).
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:221-231 — `compute(_extractArchiveStreaming, ...)` runs extraction in an isolate, but the `finally` block at 225-231 deletes the archive *even on success*; if extraction partially succeeded and a later step fails, the user cannot retry from the cached archive and must re-download 180 MB — delete only on confirmed failure, or keep the archive until extraction is verified.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:242-268 — `_extractArchiveStreaming` uses `Directory.systemTemp.createTempSync('lineguide_extract')` and `deleteSync(recursive: true)` in `finally`; on a concurrent second invocation (two downloads racing) the shared `'lineguide_extract'` prefix is fine (createTempSync uniquifies), but if `deleteSync` throws mid-delete the `catch (_) {}` at 264-266 leaks hundreds of MB of temp tar data with no cleanup on next launch — record the temp dir path in the log service for a startup sweep.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:280-284 — `_downloadFile` short-circuits when `localPath` already exists (`onProgress?.call(1.0); return;`) without any size/hash check, so a *previous partial* file (e.g. from a killed run) is reported as fully downloaded and the caller proceeds to extract it — verify existing-file size/hash before the early return, mirroring `fileProblem`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:298 — `request.headers.set(HttpHeaders.acceptEncodingHeader, 'identity')` is set *after* `getUrl` but the request may already have been sent on some platforms; more importantly, if the server ignores it and gzips, the code at 290 has `autoUncompress = false` so raw gzip bytes land in the archive file — the SHA-256 check at 205 would then fail on a *good* archive, forcing an endless re-download loop — detect `Content-Encoding: gzip` on the response and either decode or fail with a clear message (the sibling service handles this at model_download_service.dart:645-651; this file does not).
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:301-333 — the redirect handling block only runs when `statusCode != 200`; a 3xx response *with* `location` is followed, but a 3xx *without* `location` falls through to `throw Exception('Download failed: HTTP ${response.statusCode}')` at 333 after `drain` — correct, but a 303/308 with a `location` is treated identically to 302 (no method/body semantics), and a redirect to a non-HTTPS target throws *after* `client.close()` at 309 has already been called inside the branch, then `finally` at 362-364 calls `client.close()` again — double-close of an `HttpClient` is harmless in dart:io but the branch-level `close()` at 309 makes the recursive `_downloadFile` call at 327 use a *closed* client for the next hop's request creation... actually the recursion creates its own client, so the real defect is: `client.close()` at 309 closes the client *before* the recursive call completes, which is fine, but the `finally` at 362 then closes an already-closed client — no crash, but the `redirectsLeft` bookkeeping at 322-328 recurses with `redirectsLeft - 1` while the *outer* caller's counter is separate, so a redirect chain that bounces between two hosts consumes the budget correctly — no exploitable defect; the genuine issue is that `response.drain<void>()` at 308/332 is awaited *before* `close()`, good — downgrade this whole item to: the non-200 fall-through at 333 throws without draining when `redirectUrl == null` and `statusCode` is 3xx, leaking the connection — move `drain` before the throw.

- [low] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:157-164 — `clearCache` deletes the models dir then immediately recreates it, but if `delete` throws (file in use) the `create` never runs and the app is left with no models dir; subsequent `modelsDir` getter recreates it lazily, so impact is low — wrap delete+create in a try/catch that still recreates on failure.

- [medium] lib/data/services/ocr_highlight_matcher.dart:96-149 — locate() assumes lines list is page-ordered and contiguous; if callers pass lines from multiple pages or out of order, the backward walk (startIdx) and forward extension (i) can highlight rects on the wrong page — document/enforce ordering invariant at the call boundary
- [low] lib/data/services/ocr_highlight_matcher.dart:158-193 — bestMatch returns `(index: bestIdx, score: best)` where bestIdx tracks the best weak candidate but the strong-match early return (line 186) returns immediately without updating bestIdx — if a strong match exists at i but a later weak candidate has higher score, the strong match is correctly returned first; however if strongScore is unreachable and best < minScore, returns null even though a candidate scored exactly minScore-epsilon — acceptable per spec, no defect.

lib/features/recording_studio/recording_studio_screen.dart — findings: 7
- [medium] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:196-217 — async existence scan races the memoized list: `_scanFileExistence` is invoked from `_memoEntries` (line 84) during `build()`, and the `setState` at 212 replaces the whole `_fileResolved` map after awaits; if the user changes the character filter or a recording is deleted while the scan is in flight, the map is keyed only by recording id so stale `true`/`false` values for recordings no longer in the list are harmless, but a recording deleted mid-scan can re-add a resolved entry for a row already removed from the notifier — consequence: a deleted take can briefly reappear playable (tap does nothing or plays a ghost path) — smallest safe fix: capture the scanned key inside the async closure and bail (`if (key != _scannedKey) return;`) before calling `setState`.
- [medium] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:604 — `_resolveRecordingPath` trusts `recording.localPath` as an absolute path and `File(...).exists()` on it; a recording row whose `localPath` came from another device/cloud sync (e.g. `/data/...` on iOS or a path with `../`) is probed directly — consequence: on a shared/synced device this can read outside the app's recordings directory (path traversal via a crafted `local_path` column) — smallest safe fix: resolve against the app's Documents/recordings dir (join basename only, reject paths containing `..` or absolute prefixes from other containers) before any `exists()`/`lengthSync()`.
- [medium] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:670 — `File(resolvedPath).lengthSync()` is called on the UI isolate after the async resolver; a large or network-mounted file (or a file deleted between `exists()` and `lengthSync()`) throws `FileSystemException` synchronously inside `_playRecording`'s try? — no: line 670 is *outside* the try block that starts at 686 — consequence: an unhandled synchronous exception crashes the app on a TOCTOU-deleted file — smallest safe fix: move the size probe inside the try/catch (or use `await File(...).length()`).
- [low] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:648 — `recording.scriptLineId.substring(0, 8)` will throw a RangeError if a recording row has a `scriptLineId` shorter than 8 chars (e.g. a legacy/empty id) — consequence: tapping play on such a row crashes instead of logging — smallest safe fix: use `substring(0, min(8, id.length))` or log the full id.
- [low] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:619 — cache-path fallback matches `path.contains(recording.scriptLineId)`; a scriptLineId that is a short/empty string matches unrelated cache files — consequence: the wrong cached take may be played for a row whose id is a substring of another filename — smallest safe fix: compare basenames exactly (`p.basename(path) == filename`) and drop the `contains` clause.
- [info] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:557 — `recording.id.startsWith('cache_')` is used as a trust marker to skip cloud deletion; verify the id prefix convention is set by this app only and not by server-side data (a malicious/foreign row with id `cache_...` would silently skip cloud cleanup) — verify it is not committed/derived from untrusted input.
- [low] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_history_screen.dart:99-102 — `avgScore` divides by `sessions.length` guarded only by `isEmpty` on the *fold* input; when `sessions` is non-empty this is fine, but the ternary computes `sessions.fold(...)/sessions.length` where the guard checks `sessions.isEmpty` — actually correct; no division-by-zero. No finding.
- [low] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_history_screen.dart:276-279 — `_formatDate` uses `now.difference(dt)`; a `startedAt` in the future (clock skew / bad data) yields a negative `diff`, so `diff.inDays == 0` may be false and the branch falls through to `'${dt.month}/${dt.day}'` — cosmetic only; no crash. No finding.

- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:3296-3322 — `_scrollToCurrentLine` reads `ref` inside a post-frame callback scheduled after unmount — the callback body checks `mounted` only after `WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback` has already captured the closure, and the fallback branch reads `ref.read(currentLineIndexProvider)` — if the widget unmounted between scheduling and the frame, Riverpod throws "Using ref after unmount" (the exact crash class the file's own comments at 198-199 and 503-505 document) — guard `mounted` before scheduling or capture the index before the callback

- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:606 — `_migrateVoiceConfig`'s catch block calls `ScaffoldMessenger.of(context)` and `Navigator`-adjacent UI after an `if (!mounted) return;` guard that checks the State's `mounted`, but the async gap began before the dialog/toast context was captured — if the widget was disposed mid-await the messenger lookup can throw — smallest safe fix: capture the `ScaffoldMessenger`/`BuildContext` before the `await` and use the captured messenger, or guard with `context.mounted` (Flutter 3.7+ `BuildContext.mounted`) instead of State `mounted`.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:676-700 — same pattern in `_migrateCastMembers`: two `ScaffoldMessenger.of(context)` calls after async gaps guarded only by State `mounted` — same consequence and fix.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:309-315 — `_setCharacterLocale`'s `onChanged` callback calls `setState` after `await VoiceConfigService.instance.setLocale(...)` with no `mounted` check at all — if the user closes the screen while the await is pending, `setState` on a disposed State throws — smallest safe fix: add `if (!mounted) return;` before the `setState`.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:137 — subtitle builds `${_localeOptions[_charLocales[char.name]] ?? _charLocales[char.name]}`: when the stored locale is a key not present in `_localeOptions` (e.g. a locale saved by another screen or an older build), the raw locale string is shown instead of a friendly label — cosmetic only, but the fallback direction is permissive display of unvalidated data — smallest safe fix: fall back to the raw value only when it is a known locale code, else omit the segment.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:243-250 — `_toggleGender` persists the new gender via `VoiceConfigService.instance.setGender` before updating in-memory state, and the service call's returned Future is not awaited/checked — if the persist fails silently the UI shows the new gender while storage keeps the old one, and the next reload reverts the toggle with no user feedback — smallest safe fix: `await` the call (make `_toggleGender` async) and surface failure via the existing toast path.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:554-589 — `_applyRename` is `async` but its callers (`FilledButton.onPressed` at 351, ListTile `onTap` at 400-402) invoke it without awaiting; exceptions from `_migrateVoiceConfig`/`_migrateCastMembers` escape as unhandled async errors — smallest safe fix: wrap the body's error handling (already partly done) and have callers `unawaited(...)` or await inside a try/catch at the call site.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:732 — `existingGenders.putIfAbsent(renamedTo, () => carried)` only carries the gender when `renamedTo` is absent; if the new name already had a (possibly stale/default) entry the old gender silently wins — direction-of-failure check: the rename path prefers the pre-existing entry over the user's actual gender — smallest safe fix: overwrite with `existingGenders[renamedTo] = carried` when `carried != null`.
- [info] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:749 — gender fallback defaults to `CharacterGender.female` for any character with no recorded gender — a permissive default for a personal-preference control; consider a neutral `nonGendered` default so unknown characters don't get a gendered voice assignment.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/cloud_sync_dialog.dart:217 — `final line = diff.cloud ?? diff.local!;` uses a non-null assertion on `diff.local`; `LineDiff` allows both `local` and `cloud` to be null (constructor at 13 has no non-null invariant), so a future `DiffType.unchanged`-adjacent path or a refactored caller passing `LineDiff(type: ..., local: null, cloud: null)` would crash here — smallest safe fix: guard `if (diff.local == null && diff.cloud == null) return const SizedBox.shrink();` before the assertion.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/cloud_sync_dialog.dart:231 — the icon color ternary chain only handles green/red/orange backgrounds; for any other `bgColor` (currently unreachable, but the chain is fragile) the icon falls through to `Colors.orange` implicitly via the final else — harmless today, but the expression should derive the icon color from `bgColor` directly (e.g. a map) rather than re-deriving via chained equality against `withValues(alpha: 0.1)` results, which are not `const`-comparable and rely on identical computed values — smallest safe fix: pass the base color alongside `bgColor` and use it directly.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/cloud_sync_dialog.dart:284-290 — `_sameList` compares element-by-element with `!=` on `String`s; this is correct for `String`, but the helper is only used for `multiCharacters` and duplicates the logic that `listEquals` from flutter/foundation already provides — not a defect, noted only because the loop's early `return false` on length mismatch is the only guard; no fix needed.

- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:248,260-268 — split slider allows splitAt == dialogueLines.length (max = length-1 but onChanged rounds v up to length when slider value exceeds max? no — actual defect: Slider min:1, max:(dialogueLines.length - 1) with divisions length-2 is consistent, but when dialogueLines.length <= 2 the button is disabled only in build; _splitScene is still reachable via the Split button's onPressed being null-guarded — however the real defect is at 283/292: dialogueLines[splitAt] with splitAt == length-1 is valid, but dialogueLines[splitAt - 1] with splitAt == 0 is impossible since min is 1 — the reachable defect is line 311: dialogueLines[splitAt].orderIndex is passed as splitAtOrderIndex, then _performSplit at 411-412 looks up orderIndex equality which can match a line outside this scene when orderIndex values are duplicated across scenes, splitting at the wrong line — consequence: split inserts the scene boundary at a different act/scene's line, corrupting scene ranges — fix: match by line id (or index within dialogueLines) instead of orderIndex equality.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:411-414 — _performSplit silently returns when no line matches the orderIndex (splitLineIdx < 0), but the dialog has already popped and the user sees no feedback — consequence: split appears to succeed but nothing changes, silent no-op — fix: surface the failure (e.g., SnackBar) or resolve the index before showing the dialog.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:419-421 — start/end are clamped from stale scene bounds, then splitAt is clamped into [start, end]; if the scene's stored range is stale (endLineIndex < startLineIndex after deletions), firstHalfLines/secondHalfLines at 424-425 can be empty or inverted — consequence: split produces a zero-line or negative-range scene pair, corrupting the script state — fix: validate start < end (and splitAt in (start, end)) before performing the split and bail with user feedback.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:455,468 — `characters: firstChars.toList()..sort()` sorts in place after toList, but the cascade applies to the temporary list from toList() which is fine; however `..sort()` on the result of toList() is correct — no defect here; skip.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:490 — merge union `{...scene.characters, ...nextScene.characters}` loses duplicate-free ordering but that's intended; the real defect: merged scene keeps `description: scene.description` and drops nextScene.description silently — consequence: data loss of the second scene's description on merge with no confirmation — fix: concatenate descriptions or note the loss in the dialog.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:503-504 — _performMerge mutates `scenes[sceneIndex] = merged` after removeAt(sceneIndex + 1); if sceneIndex + 1 >= scenes.length this throws RangeError — guarded only by the UI condition at 170 (`index < script.scenes.length - 1`), so any programmatic or stale-state call path (e.g., rebuild after another mutation) crashes — consequence: uncaught RangeError in production flow — fix: guard sceneIndex + 1 < scenes.length inside _performMerge and return early.

- [low] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:518-520 — _truncate subtracts 3 from maxLen but callers pass 50; if maxLen <= 3 substring throws RangeError — consequence: crash for tiny maxLen values (currently unreachable with 50, latent) — fix: guard maxLen > 3 or use substring(0, maxLen.clamp(0, text.length)).

ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift — findings: 4
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:100-110 — no validation of caller-supplied `url`/`destinationPath` at the method-channel trust boundary — a Dart-side (or compromised plugin channel) caller can point the native downloader at arbitrary URLs (including internal/LAN hosts, i.e. SSRF from the app process) and write the downloaded bytes to any absolute path the app can reach (e.g. overwriting Documents/Library files or clobbering an existing file at a chosen path) — validate scheme/host allowlist for `url` and confine `destinationPath` under a fixed app container directory before creating directories or starting the task.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:113-115 — `try?` swallows directory-creation failure and the code proceeds to start the download anyway — if the destination directory cannot be created (disk full, sandbox denial, bad path), the transfer completes and the move in `didFinishDownloadingTo` then fails, surfacing a generic "Failed to save file" error after a multi-GB download instead of failing fast — drop `try?`, return an error to the caller when `createDirectory` fails before scheduling the task.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:118-126 — cancel-then-start race with no state reconciliation: the old task is cancelled but its persisted record is immediately overwritten by `persistDownloadRecord(info)` for the new URL/destination — if the cancelled task's error callback (line 282-289) fires after the new record is written, `scheduleRetry`/resume-data handling runs against the *new* download's state, potentially writing the old task's resume data over the new download's blob and retrying the wrong transfer — key resume data and retry state by a per-task identifier (or remove the record before cancelling and re-add only after the old task's callbacks have drained).
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:134-141 — `cancelDownload` only cancels when the task is in the in-memory `activeDownloads` map; a download that completed/failed while the app was terminated has no in-memory entry, so cancel silently succeeds (`result(true)`) without removing the persisted record or the resume blob — a later `startDownload` for the same modelId can then resume from stale resume data the user believed they had cancelled — call `removeDownloadRecord(modelId)` and delete `resumePath` unconditionally in `cancelDownload`, not only inside the `if let info` branch.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:150-163 — `startTask` reads resume data from `resumePath(info.destinationPath)` and resumes without verifying the resume data belongs to this URL/model — resume blobs are keyed only by destination path, so a cancelled-then-restarted download at the same destination (or a record restored post-relaunch whose URL changed) resumes a transfer for a different source URL, silently downloading the wrong content into the user's destination — store the source URL (or modelId) inside the persisted resume record and verify it matches `info.url` before using `withResumeData`.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:167-188 — `scheduleRetry` mutates `info.retryCount` on a *copy* obtained from the dictionary (`guard var info = activeDownloads[modelId]`) and writes it back, but the retry closure at 183-187 re-reads `activeDownloads[modelId]` and calls `startTask(&info)` without re-checking `retryCount > maxAutoRetries` — if a second failure callback arrives while a retry is already scheduled (e.g. the cancelled-task error from a `startDownload` re-issue), the backoff delay and the retry counter can double-fire, producing more than `maxAutoRetries` attempts and overlapping `asyncAfter` closures that each restart the task — guard the retry closure on a monotonic attempt token stored in `DownloadInfo` and bail if a retry for this modelId is already pending.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:197-201 — `didFinishDownloadingTo` drops the completed transfer when neither memory nor disk state can be reconstructed, but the temp file at `location` is still consumed by the system — the comment at 55-63 says this was the historical bug; the current code still returns silently (no `onDownloadError` to Dart) when `restoredDownloadInfo` fails (e.g. UserDefaults record missing/corrupt), so a multi-GB download can again complete and be silently discarded with no UI signal — emit an `onDownloadError` invokeMethod (or persist the record *before* `task.resume()` so reconstruction can never fail) instead of a bare `return`.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:203-208 — move-to-destination is not atomic and the success path removes the resume blob *before* confirming the move persisted — `try FileManager.default.moveItem` is inside the same `do` as the success cleanup, but if the process is terminated between the move and the Dart callback, the destination file exists with no record and no resume data, and the Dart side never receives `onDownloadComplete` for this modelId — write the completion record (or invoke the Dart callback) before clearing resume state, and prefer moving to a temp name + atomic rename.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:210-216 — `attributesOfItem(atPath:)` result is force-cast through `as? Int` with a `?? 0` fallback and the size is reported to Dart even when the attribute lookup failed — a 0-byte size is reported as success for a multi-GB file, and Dart-side logic keyed on `size` (dedupe, progress display, storage quota) cannot distinguish "unknown" from "empty" — use `URLResourceValues`/`onError` handling and omit or mark the size field when the stat fails rather than defaulting to 0.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:265-270 — `didCompleteWithError` returns early on `error == nil` and on `NSURLErrorCancelled`, but a cancelled task that had already written partial data does not get its resume data saved here (cancel path relies on the `cancelDownload` handler at 138, which only runs for in-memory entries) — combined with the post-relaunch case above, a user cancel after relaunch leaves the partial transfer with neither resume data removal nor retry scheduling, and the persisted record stays forever — route cancel handling through a single path that always reconciles persisted state regardless of which callback observes the cancellation.
- [low] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:93,155,171,182,199,218,284,287 — `NSLog` of modelId/URL/error strings into the unified system log — destination paths and URLs (which may embed signed query params or user-identifying slugs) persist in device logs readable by any app with log access on that device and in sysdiagnose dumps — log only the modelId hash/length and error domain/code, not full URLs or destination paths.
- [low] ios/Runner/AudioAnalysisPlugin.swift:44-47 — `try? AVAudioFile(forReading:)` failure returns `result(nil)` with no error surfaced to Dart — the Dart side cannot distinguish "file missing/corrupt" from "measured silence", and the documented contract ("nil = no normalization") makes a corrupt-file silent fallback indistinguishable from a legitimate skip — return a distinct error code (or a `available:false` field) so the caller can decide, rather than a bare nil.
- [low] ios/Runner/AudioAnalysisPlugin.swift:50-55 — `AVAudioPCMBuffer(pcmFormat:frameCapacity:)` and `file.read(into:)` failures also collapse to `result(nil)` with no diagnostic — same silent-fallback class as above; a partially-read buffer (read throws mid-way) still falls through to the RMS loop at 67-77 using whatever frames were decoded, producing a wrong-but-plausible loudness value for a truncated file — check `buffer.frameLength == frameCount` (or the read's returned frame count) before computing RMS, and surface read errors instead of nil.
- [low] ios/Runner/AudioAnalysisPlugin.swift:79-82 — RMS/peak are computed over `buffer.frameLength` frames (`n`) but the loop at 71-76 iterates `0..<n` over `floatChannelData` regardless of whether the buffer was fully populated — combined with the partial-read case above, a short read yields an RMS over fewer frames than the file actually contains, biasing normalization for truncated recordings — gate the computation on a full-buffer read as in the previous finding.
- [info] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:65-75 — UserDefaults-backed persistence of download records under a single fixed key — records are rewritten wholesale on every start/cancel; concurrent `startDownload`/`cancelDownload` calls (both arrive on the main queue via `delegateQueue: .main`, but the retry `asyncAfter` closure and delegate callbacks also mutate the same dict) can interleave read-modify-write on UserDefaults and lose a cancel — serialize all record mutations through one queue or a dedicated serial accessor; verify it is not committed with real URLs if any sample data is present (none visible in this file).

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:336-339 — `pruneScheduled` is read/written from multiple threads without synchronization (checked on the caller thread at 338, set inside the utility-queue block at 339, and it is a `static var` Bool, not atomic) — two concurrent first calls can both pass the guard and schedule duplicate prune passes, and the flag can be torn/stale across threads — guard the check-and-set with a lock (or use `NSLock`/`os_unfair_lock` around the read-modify-write) so only one prune pass is ever scheduled.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:171,173-177 — cache-hit path returns the cached WAV path without re-checking that the file is still valid/complete after the LRU prune pass may have removed it between `fileExists` and the caller opening it (TOCTOU on the cache entry) — a caller can receive a path whose file was just pruned, producing a failed playback that looks like a synthesis bug rather than a cache miss — re-stat the file (or hold the prune until the current synthesis completes) so a hit cannot race a concurrent prune removal.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:203-268 — `withCheckedThrowingContinuation` wraps `synthQueue.async` but never checks `Task.isCancelled` and the continuation is resumed from a detached GCD block, so a cancelled Swift-concurrency task still runs the full MLX synthesis and its result is discarded — wasted GPU/CPU work and battery on every cancelled request (e.g., user skips a line mid-synthesis) — check `Task.isCancelled` before enqueuing and bail out early (or use `withTaskCancellationHandler` to skip the queued block).
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:200 — language is derived from a single-character prefix `voice.hasPrefix("a")`, so any voice id starting with 'a' is treated as en-US and everything else as en-GB; a future voice id whose prefix does not encode locale (or a typo'd voice string) silently picks the wrong language/accent for synthesis — derive language from an explicit per-voice table (or validate the voice id against `availableVoices` before mapping) instead of a one-letter heuristic.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:284-291 — `vDSP_vclip`/`vsmul`/`vfix16` write into `scaled`/`pcm` declared with `var` but the pointers are taken via `&lo`/`&hi`/`&scale` on scalars and the destination arrays are passed by `UnsafePointer`-style first argument without `withUnsafeBufferPointer` scoping; if the compiler promotes these to registers the vDSP calls can read stale/uninitialized memory — wrap each array in `withUnsafeMutableBufferPointer` (or use `vDSP`'s typed API) so the pointers are guaranteed valid for the duration of the call.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:139,366 — `NSLog` messages include no user content, but the prune log at 366 prints only counts; no secret leakage found — no action needed beyond confirming no PII is ever added to these log lines.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertEmbeddings.swift:15-20 — `weights[...]!` force-unwraps four dictionary lookups; a safetensors file missing any of the `bert.embeddings.*` keys crashes the process at model load with no diagnostic — replace `!` with `guard let ... else { fatalError("missing key ...") }` (or throw a typed error) so the failure names the absent weight.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertEmbeddings.swift:29-30 — `layerNorm.bias![0...] = ...` force-unwraps optional MLX parameter storage and assigns a whole range in place; if the vendored `LayerNorm` lazily allocates these on first use the unwrap can crash — verify the parameters are materialized before the in-place assign (or use the non-optional update API) so a lazy-parameter layout cannot crash at load.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/InstanceNorm1d.swift:68-92 — training-mode fallback resolves to the permissive branch when running stats are absent: in eval mode with trackRunningStats=false the code silently recomputes batch statistics instead of erroring, so a checkpoint built for eval-time running stats silently produces different (batch-dependent) normalization — consequence: inference output depends on the batch composition, non-reproducible results and silent divergence from the trained model; smallest safe fix: raise/fatalError when !trackRunningStats && !training instead of falling through to the training path.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/InstanceNorm1d.swift:76-81 — running-stat update uses `momentum` as (1-momentum) blend but PyTorch-style momentum convention is E_new = (1-momentum)*E_old + momentum*E_batch; here `overallMean` is the mean of the batch means, which is only correct when reduceDims includes axis 0 — for 3D input (B, C, L) with featureDim=C the reduce set excludes axis 0, so the running stats are updated with a per-batch-mean rather than the full-batch statistic — consequence: runningMean/runningVar drift incorrectly across inference steps, corrupting eval-mode normalization; smallest safe fix: reduce over all axes including batch when updating running stats (or document the intended axis).
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/InstanceNorm1d.swift:60-61 — `featureDim` computed as `dims.count - getNoBatchDim()` assumes getNoBatchDim() is a fixed offset from the end; for InstanceNorm1d (getNoBatchDim()==2) with 2D input (C, L) featureDim resolves to dims[0] (C) which is correct, but with 3D input (B, C, L) it resolves to dims[1] (C) — correct only because ndim==3; if a caller passes ndim>3 the index silently points at a non-feature axis — consequence: silent wrong-axis normalization for unexpected ranks; smallest safe fix: validate input.ndim == expected rank in checkInputDim before computing featureDim.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/Interpolate.swift:23-27 — `size`/`scaleFactor` mutual-exclusion check uses `!= nil` on optional arrays; an empty array `[]` passes the "defined" check but produces outputSize=[] at line 30/40, then interpolate1d is called with size=outputSize[0] which traps (index out of range) — consequence: crash on empty-but-non-nil argument instead of a clear validation error; smallest safe fix: treat empty arrays as undefined (size?.isEmpty == false) or validate outputSize non-empty before indexing.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/Interpolate.swift:32-38 — scaleFactor shorter than spatialDims (and count != 1) is used directly with `factors[i]` indexing past the end — consequence: runtime index-out-of-range crash for malformed scale factors; smallest safe fix: validate scaleFactor.count == spatialDims || scaleFactor.count == 1 before use.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/Interpolate.swift:40 — `size` longer than spatialDims is silently truncated by the loop over spatialDims (extra entries ignored) while a shorter size (count>1 but < spatialDims) would crash at factors-style indexing in the caller path — consequence: inconsistent validation of user-supplied size; smallest safe fix: require size.count == spatialDims || size.count == 1 with an explicit error otherwise.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/Interpolate.swift:79-88 — when alignCorners==true and outputSize==1 the true-branch is skipped (guard requires outputSize>1) and the else branch computes x with the non-aligned formula then adds the half-pixel shift only when alignCorners != true; for alignCorners==true && outputSize==1 x ends up as inputWidth/outputSize scaled grid without the align_corners semantics — consequence: single-output-sample linear interpolation diverges from PyTorch's align_corners=True degenerate case (which maps output position 0 to input position 0); smallest safe fix: special-case alignCorners==true && outputSize==1 to x=[0] (or map to input center per PyTorch's actual behavior).
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/Interpolate.swift:97-103 — comment documents a past audio bug fix (negative-index wrap clamp); the clamp at 104-106 is present in the code, so no live defect — no action needed.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Generator.swift:82 — MLX.product(MLXArray(upsampleRates)[(i + 1)...]).item() on an empty suffix (i+1 == count is guarded, but a 1-element list makes the slice the full array — fine; however if upsampleRates has length 1 the else-branch at 105 is taken and strideF0 is never computed, so no issue) — no defect; withdrawn.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Generator.swift:55-57 — same force-unwrap pattern for decoder.generator.ups.* keys; a wrong checkpoint variant crashes with no context — same fix as above.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Generator.swift:131-133 — same force-unwrap pattern for decoder.generator.conv_post.* keys — same fix as above.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:29 — dimIn + 2 is passed as the AdainResBlk1d input dim while the caller concatenates [asr, F0, NProcessed] along axis 1; if F0 and N each contribute more than one channel the +2 constant silently mismatches the actual feature width and the weight-multiply fails or broadcasts wrongly — derive the input dim from the concatenated tensor shape or assert it.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:31-33 — the three decode blocks are constructed with dimIn 1024 + 2 + 64 but the loop at 86-95 feeds x whose channel count changes after each block's upsample; the constant is only correct for the first block unless the reference model guarantees it — verify against the checkpoint's expected shapes or compute per-block dims.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:74 — F0Curve.reshaped([F0Curve.shape[0], 1, F0Curve.shape[1]]) assumes a 2-D input; a 3-D F0 (batch, 1, time) silently reshapes to the wrong rank and swappedAxes then transposes garbage — validate ndim before reshaping.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:77 — N.reshaped([N.shape[0], 1, N.shape[1]]) has the same rank assumption as line 74 — validate ndim.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:84-95 — res starts true and flips to false after the first upsample block, but the loop condition only checks block.upsampleType != "none"; if an earlier block has upsampleType "none" while a later one upsamples, res is flipped in the wrong order and the skip-connection concatenation at 88 runs with stale residuals — re-derive res from the block list order rather than a mutable flag.

## Coverage
ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift — findings: 12
ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Generator.swift — findings: 17
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:94-97 — numFrames computed via integer division `(xArray.shape[0] - nFft) / hopLen` with no rounding-up; when the padded input length leaves a partial final frame the last `hopLen` samples are silently dropped from the STFT — consequence: reconstructed audio via `mlxIstft` is shorter/truncated and phase/magnitude frames misalign with the original signal tail — smallest safe fix: compute `numFrames = 1 + ceil((xArray.shape[0] - nFft) / hopLen)` (or `(len - nFft + hopLen - 1) / hopLen`) so trailing samples are included.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:130-132 — `mlxIstft` pads `w` with zeros when `w.shape[0] < winLen` but never truncates when `w.shape[0] > winLen`; the overlap-add then uses a window longer than the frame stride — consequence: `windowSum` accumulates over a window that extends past each frame boundary, producing incorrect normalization and audible artifacts in reconstructed audio whenever a caller supplies a window larger than `winLength` — smallest safe fix: slice `w = w[0 ..< winLen]` after the pad branch (mirroring the STFT-side handling) before building `wSquared`.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:142 — `totalWsquared` built as `Array(repeating: wSquared, count: t / winLen)`; `t / winLen` truncates, so when `t` is not an exact multiple of `winLen` the per-position window sums are zero-padded (lines 159-163) rather than tiled — consequence: positions beyond the last full tile divide by a zero (or partial) window sum, yielding NaN/inf or attenuated samples in the reconstructed waveform — smallest safe fix: tile with `count: (t + winLen - 1) / winLen` (or clamp `windowSumArray` slicing to the padded length) so every output position has a nonzero normalizer.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:63,68,84,96,122,127 — several failure paths call `fatalError` (window type unsupported, input too short, invalid pad mode) inside vendored DSP code — consequence: a malformed caller input (e.g. audio shorter than `nFft`, or a non-"hann" window string) aborts the whole app process instead of surfacing a typed error — smallest safe fix: replace `fatalError` with thrown errors/`Result` so callers can handle invalid input gracefully.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:255-260 — `callAsFunction` stores `magnitude`/`phase` on the instance but `inverse` is invoked with the freshly computed `mag`/`ph`, making the stored properties dead state that retains large MLX arrays — consequence: needless memory retention of full spectrograms per `MLXSTFT` instance across calls (relevant for long-running TTS sessions) — smallest safe fix: drop the stored `magnitude`/`phase` properties (or make them locals) since no inlined code reads them.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:136-138 — `precondition(winLen % hopLen == 0)` guards the overlap-add aliasing; with the shipped defaults (800/200) it holds, but a config change to a non-divisible pair crashes at runtime rather than being caught at init — consequence: misconfiguration surfaces as a runtime abort in production inference instead of a construction-time failure — smallest safe fix: validate `winLength % hopLength == 0` in `init` (and in `mlxStft` for the forward pass) so bad configs fail fast at setup.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/SineGen.swift:47 — `radValues[...] = radValues[...] + randIni` adds a full `normal`-shaped tensor (`[f0Values.shape[0], f0Values.shape[2]]`) directly onto a slice whose middle axis is 1; if `f0Values.shape[2] != randIni.shape[1]` broadcasting silently misaligns, and the assignment overwrites the slice rather than accumulating in-place safely — consequence: phase-noise injection can broadcast across the wrong axis, corrupting the generated sine phases for inputs whose third dimension differs from the noise tensor's second dimension — smallest safe fix: reshape/expand `randIni` to `radValues`' slice shape explicitly (e.g. `randIni.expandedDimensions(axis: 1)`) before the in-place add, or assert shape equality.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/SineGen.swift:45-47 — `randIni[0..., 0] = MLXArray(0.0)` zeroes only the first column of the noise tensor but the subsequent in-place add (line 47) writes into `radValues`' slice, not `randIni`; the zeroing therefore has no effect on the value actually added — consequence: the intended "no noise on first harmonic" behavior is silently not applied, changing generated waveform characteristics versus the reference implementation — smallest safe fix: apply the zeroing to the tensor that is added (e.g. build `randIni` with a zeroed first column and add that), or remove the dead write.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/SineGen.swift:67 — `MLXArray(1 ... harmonicNum + 1)` builds the harmonic range from the constructor's `harmonicNum` (default 0), so `range.shape[0] == 1` and `fn` reduces to `f0` itself; callers passing `harmonicNum > 0` get extra harmonics but the default silently produces a pure-tone generator regardless of `dim` — consequence: any caller relying on `dim` (line 29) rather than `harmonicNum` sees an off-by-one harmonic count — smallest safe fix: derive the range from `dim` (`1 ... dim`) so the two cannot diverge.

- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/DurationEncoder.swift:137-139 — the scatter/copy into `xPad` assumes `x.shape[2] <= m.shape[-1]`; if the LSTM output ever exceeds the mask's sequence length the slice assignment silently truncates or (if MLX enforces shape match) throws — consequence: a shape mismatch between encoder output and mask length produces either silent data truncation or a hard crash with no diagnostic — smallest safe fix: assert or clamp `x.shape[2]` against `m.shape[-1]` before the slice copy
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/DurationEncoder.swift:86 — `style.shape[style.shape.count - 1]` assumes style is at least 1-D and its last axis is the embedding dim; a 0-D or unexpected-rank style tensor yields a wrong broadcast target or an index-out-of-bounds — consequence: malformed style input crashes rather than being rejected — smallest safe fix: validate `style.ndim >= 1` before broadcasting
