- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:33-34 — `left` is assigned `right` (both start at 0) making the initial `left` value dead code and the first token's start timestamp computed from an uninitialized baseline — consequence: for the first phoneme token `t.start_ts` at line 60 is derived from a baseline that never reflects the BOS lead-in duration, so early token timestamps can be shifted/zero and downstream subtitle alignment drifts — smallest safe fix: initialize `left` from the intended lead-in (e.g. `left = right + 2 * max(0, dur[0] - 3)` semantics per the documented update rule) instead of the redundant `left = right` self-assignment.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:41-42 — loop `break`s when `i >= dur.count - 1` but `i` is only advanced inside the phoneme branch (line 66) and the whitespace branch (line 47/50), so a token whose phonemes are nil and whitespace is empty leaves `i` unchanged forever — consequence: with a nil-phoneme, empty-whitespace token the loop can spin without progress on subsequent iterations (or silently mis-index `dur[i]` at line 48 if a later whitespace token arrives), producing wrong timestamps or an out-of-bounds read on `dur` — smallest safe fix: advance `i` (or `continue` with an explicit increment) in the nil-phoneme/empty-whitespace path so every iteration makes progress.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:48-49 — `dur[i]` is indexed after the guard only checked `i < dur.count - 1` at loop entry, but `i` was incremented at line 47 before this access — consequence: when the last token is whitespace, `i` can equal `dur.count` (or `dur.count - 1` after the second increment at line 50) and `dur[i]` reads past the end, crashing the TTS run at runtime — smallest safe fix: re-check `i < dur.count` (or clamp) immediately before each `dur[i]` access.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:60,64 — `end_ts`/`start_ts` are written onto the shared `MToken` structs passed in as parameters, mutating caller-owned token state as a side effect of a static function named `preditTimestamps` (also a typo of `predict`) — consequence: callers that re-run prediction or reuse tokens get silently mutated start/end values, and the typo suggests an untested API surface — smallest safe fix: return the computed timestamps (e.g. `[(Double, Double)]`) instead of mutating the input tokens, and rename to `predictTimestamps`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/WeightLoader.swift:88 — `if key.contains("noise_convs"), key.hasSuffix(".weight")` uses Swift's implicit-Bool-coalescing comma form which is valid but obscure; combined with line 89 transposing every matching decoder conv — consequence: a decoder weight named e.g. `...noise_convs.bias` is silently passed through untransposed (correct) but any future key like `noise_convs_something.weight` also gets transposed, and the readability hazard invites wrong edits — smallest safe fix: write the condition explicitly as `if key.contains("noise_convs") && key.hasSuffix(".weight")`.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/WeightLoader.swift:36-38 — `loadWeights` loads arrays from a caller-supplied `modelPath` URL with no path/scheme validation and `try MLX.loadArrays(url:)` may deserialize untrusted model files — consequence: if `modelPath` can be influenced by untrusted input (assumption: caller passes a user-choosable URL), a crafted weight file could shape the model's behavior; verify the path is restricted to the app-bundled Kokoro weights directory — smallest safe fix: confirm the call site pins `modelPath` to a bundled/known-good location; if not, validate the URL against an allow-listed directory before loading.

- [low] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/English/FallbackNetwork/EnglishFallbackNetwork.swift:64-68 — `tokensToPhonemes` drops tokens whose id maps to no phoneme and silently skips unknown/low ids — consequence: generated phoneme strings can silently omit characters, producing wrong pronunciation output with no error surfaced; smallest safe fix: map unknown ids to a placeholder or log the dropped token id. **File 2: test/cast_member_test.dart**

This is a unit test file for models: `CastMemberModel`, `CastRole`, `Production`, `SupabaseService.generateJoinCode`. Imports from `package:castcircle/data/models/...` and `package:castcircle/data/services/supabase_service.dart` — none of which are inlined, so we can't verify the production implementations. But the tests themselves:

- Tests assert model behavior. These are real tests that could fail.
- `generateJoinCode` uniqueness test at line 155-162: generates 100 codes from a 32-char alphabet, 6 chars — expects `greaterThan(95)`. This is a probabilistic test — with 100 draws from a space of 32^6 ≈ 1.07 billion, collisions are essentially impossible. Fine, not a "cannot fail" test — it can fail in principle but practically never. Not a finding.
- Line 143-152: character set test — asserts generated codes only contain valid chars. Real test.
- Line 164-172: asserts codes never contain I/O/0/1 — but wait, the valid alphabet at line 144 is `'ABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789'` which already excludes I, O, 0, 1. So the test at 164-172 is redundant with the character-set test but still can fail if generation is broken. Not a "cannot fail" test — it asserts against a local constant `validChars`, but that constant mirrors the documented alphabet. Hmm — "TESTS THAT CANNOT FAIL: a test asserting a literal, a local constant, or a value it defines itself proves nothing and hides regressions — flag it and name the production symbol it should assert against."

Let me reconsider. The test at line 143-152 defines `validChars` locally as a string literal and asserts generated codes contain only those chars. If the production `generateJoinCode` changed its alphabet (e.g., started including '0' or 'I'), this test would fail — because the generated code would contain a char not in the local `validChars`. So it CAN fail. It's not asserting a constant equals itself; it's asserting production output against a local expectation. That's a legitimate characterization test. The local constant could drift from the production alphabet, but the test would still catch inclusion of invalid chars per the local spec. This is borderline. The instruction says "a test asserting a literal, a local constant, or a value it defines itself proves nothing". Here the test asserts production behavior against a local constant — that's not "asserting a local constant" (like `expect(1, 1)`); it's using a local constant as the expected spec. I'd say this is fine and not a finding. Similarly line 160-161 comment about collision probability.

- No secrets, no security issues. Test-only code.

If it returned 96 distinct values out of 100, it'd pass. It's a weak uniqueness check but it's a test design choice, not a production bug. I won't flag it — the instruction says report only defensible findings; a weak-but-passing probabilistic assertion in a test isn't a defect class in the checklist. Actually "TESTS THAT CANNOT FAIL" is the defect class — this test CAN fail (for sufficiently bad generators). Not flagged.)

- [medium] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:244-246 — the `p.isWithin(dir, path)` escape check is evaluated only on the *pre-join* id string via `cachePath`, but `clearCache` (line 676-684) joins `productionId` into a directory path with no `isSafePathId` validation at all — consequence: `clearCache(productionId)` with a hostile production id can delete recursively outside the cache root (`Directory(p.join(cacheDir, '../../something')).delete(recursive: true)`), an unrecoverable destructive action; smallest safe fix: call `isSafePathId(productionId)` (and re-check `p.isWithin(cacheDir, dir)`) before the delete in `clearCache`, throwing on failure.

- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:232-246 — stop() clears onSilence/onLevel callbacks and resets session state but never resets _sttChannel.onLevel, leaving the channel wired to _handleLevel after stop — a later unrelated channel event can still invoke the stale handler and fire onSilence/onLevel callbacks that were just nulled (null-safe so no crash, but the channel keeps a dangling reference to this disposed service) — set _sttChannel.onLevel = null in stop() alongside the callback clears.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:206-210 — continuous-mode restart uses Future.delayed(200ms) with only a generation guard, no _isListening re-check against a concurrent stop() that incremented _sessionGen — actually guarded by gen == _sessionGen, but stop() increments _sessionGen so a stop() during the 200ms window correctly suppresses the restart; however a NEW listen() that increments _sessionGen then immediately starts its own session can race with the stale restart's _startAppleSession() call if the new listen() has not yet reached _startAppleSession (both call _sttChannel.listen) — consequence: two native listen() calls can overlap, tearing down the fresh session and eating the first words (the exact bug the comment at 203-205 describes as fixed for the stale path) — add an explicit _isListening && gen == _sessionGen check inside the delayed callback before calling _startAppleSession (already present at 207) and additionally guard _startAppleSession entry with a per-call in-flight flag.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:36-60 — init() returns false on screenshot mode and on engine-unavailable, and listen() (161-166) treats a false init as onDone() with no error surfaced to the caller — a user-facing "listening failed" state is indistinguishable from a normal done event, so a permission denial silently ends the session — surface a distinct error callback or log category so callers can distinguish failure from completion.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:113-128 — _handleLevel mutates _smoothedLevel/_silenceStart/_hasSpeechInUtterance from a channel callback that can fire after dispose() (dispose() at 441-443 does not clear _sttChannel.onLevel) — stale level events after dispose keep writing instance state on a torn-down singleton — clear _sttChannel.onLevel in dispose().
- [info] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:44-45,53-54,58,406-407,415-434 — DebugLogService.instance.log/logError are called with message strings that embed runtime values (locale, path) — verify debug_log_service.dart's disk persistence (debug_log.txt in app documents dir, debug_log_service.dart:109) is not shipping in release builds, since STT paths and rehearsal state would otherwise be persisted to a user-readable file — confirm release builds strip or gate DebugLogService.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:132-146 — clearProduction() removes per-actor correction patterns but _correctionPatterns.removeWhere at 145 uses a closure over droppedWords/stillUsed that can evict a pattern still referenced by a *different* production's actorCorrections map (stillUsed is built from _actorCorrections.values after removal, so a correction key shared across productions whose map entry was just removed is evicted even though another production's map still holds it) — wait: stillUsed is built from surviving maps, so eviction is correct for surviving references; the real defect is that droppedWords collects keys from removed maps but _correctionPatterns may hold patterns for words whose corrections were never in the removed maps (patterns are keyed by wrong-word and populated lazily in correct() at 177), so a pattern for a word still used by a surviving production is safe, but a pattern whose only referencing map was removed is correctly evicted — no defect exhibited; skip.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:234-238 — learnFromAttempt eviction path removes the first key from map (map.keys.first) and drops its compiled pattern from _correctionPatterns, but the evicted wrong-word may still be referenced by vocab.correctionCache for the same production — the memoized cache then holds a stale bestMatch that no longer corresponds to any learned pattern, so subsequent correct() calls (177-180) apply the stale vocabulary correction instead of the learned one — clear the evicted word from the production's vocab.correctionCache when evicting from _actorCorrections.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:308-316 — _correctWithVocabulary memoizes bestMatch into vocab.correctionCache keyed by the recognized word, but the cache is cleared wholesale (313-315) when it hits _correctionCacheLimit (4000) rather than evicting oldest — a burst of 4000 distinct words wipes all memoized corrections including recently-used ones, causing a recompute storm on the main isolate during live rehearsal — use an LRU-style eviction instead of clear().
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:496-504 — _tokenize lowercases and strips non-word chars but does not strip apostrophes inside words (the _nonWordSpaceRe pattern keeps apostrophes), so "'tis" tokenizes as "'tis" while buildFromScript's importantWords path (72-81) adds words containing "'" — consistent, but _expectedWordSet (278-283) uses the same tokenizer while _correctAgainstExpected (366-421) uses a different normalization (toLowerCase + _nonWordRe strip, which DOES remove apostrophes) — the two normalization paths disagree on apostrophe-bearing words, so a word like "o'er" can be aligned as expected in one path and stripped in the other, producing a wrong LCS alignment — unify on one tokenizer.

- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:114 — saveMetadata dereferences `supa.currentUser!` — if the uploader became ready at check time but the user signed out (or the session expired) before the metadata write, this throws a null-check operator crash inside the upload success path, losing the just-completed upload's metadata and aborting the queue loop — guard with a null check and log/skip instead of `!`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:470 — backoff delay uses `2 << nextRetry.retryCount.clamp(0, 4)` — `clamp` on an `int` returns `int` so the shift is fine, but the delay is computed from the FIRST failed job only; with many failed jobs of differing retryCount the timer fires at the smallest delay and re-adds ALL failed jobs, resetting nothing — acceptable, but note the retry budget check at 421 uses `< 5` while the log at 425 says `/5`, consistent; no fix needed — actually this is informational only, dropping it.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:249 — `queuedKeys.add(key)` on a `Set` built from a collection-for returns the add result correctly, but the set is built from BOTH `_pending` and `_failed` keys before the restore loop, so a restored job whose key matches a live failed job is silently dropped even though the live failed job may be an older take — direction of failure favors the live job, which is the safer choice; no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:388 — `file.lengthSync()` on the UI/main isolate inside the processing loop — synchronous file I/O on the main thread for every upload attempt; on large recordings this janks the UI — move to async `file.length()` or compute in a background isolate.

- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:288 — `results.any((r) => r != ConnectivityResult.none)` treats ANY connectivity event's result list as authoritative; on `connectivity_plus` the stream can emit a list where a VPN/ethernet entry coexists with none — fine — but the real issue: `hasConnection` is computed from the EVENT's results, not from a fresh check, so a stale event replayed at startup with `none` while the device is actually online will not kick the queue; the restore path at 293 covers this only if `_pending` is non-empty AND `_processing` is false — a failed-queue-only restore (pending empty, failed non-empty) never gets a connectivity kick and waits for the 30s timer that only starts inside `_processQueue` when the uploader is not ready — actually `_processQueue` is only called from connectivity events and the restore-then-kick at 294; if pending is empty and failed is non-empty, no kick happens and failed jobs sit until the next connectivity event. [medium] — failed-only restored queues never re-kick processing after restore.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:462-465 — after `_processing = false`, if `_pending` is non-empty the code schedules a microtask `_processQueue` and returns — but the `_failed` retry timer scheduling at 468 is then SKIPPED for this pass; if the queue alternates between pending and failed states, the backoff timer can be repeatedly postponed — bounded by the microtask re-entry which will reach 468 on the next pass when pending is empty; acceptable. - [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:419 — `job.retryCount++` mutates the job AFTER `_persist()` at 409 already serialized the pre-increment state; the next `_persist()` at 428 or 401 captures the incremented value — but if the process dies between 409 and 428, the persisted retryCount is one lower than in-memory, under-counting retries across restarts — however `fromJson` at 73 resets retryCount to 0 on restore anyway, so the persisted value is irrelevant after restart; the comment at 70-73 documents this as intentional. - [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:73 — `retryCount: 0` on restore gives every persisted job a FULL retry budget on every relaunch — a job that failed 5 times across 5 app restarts never exhausts its budget and is retried forever, and the `onGaveUp` callback never fires — the comment frames this as intentional ("failure may have been transient"), but the direction of failure is permissive: a permanently-bad job (e.g. corrupted local audio metadata, wrong path) is retried indefinitely across restarts, burning battery/bandwidth and never surfacing to the user via `onGaveUp`. [medium] — infinite cross-restart retry loop for poison jobs; consider persisting retryCount or a max-lifetime cap.

No finding.

lib/data/services/vision_ocr_channel.dart — clean
lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart — findings: 10
- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:31-48 — WAV chunk-walk can loop forever on malformed chunk sizes — `size` is read from the file and `offset = body + size` can jump backwards or to a value that keeps `offset + 8 <= bytes.length` true forever (e.g. a chunk whose size field makes `body + size` land back inside the same region), so `prepend` (line 64) hangs the caller's isolate on attacker/hostile or corrupted input — bound the walk (e.g. require `offset` to strictly increase, or cap iterations at `bytes.length`).
- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:76,79 — `frameSize` can be 0 or negative, making `padBytes % frameSize` throw — `_frameSize` returns `frame > 0 ? frame : 2` only when the fmt chunk is found, but if the walk at 110-117 never finds a fmt chunk it returns 2 (safe); however if a fmt chunk reports `channels * (bits ~/ 8)` as 0 the guard fixes it — the real gap is that `padBytes % frameSize` at 79 is computed before checking `padBytes > 0`, and a `frameSize` of 0 from a caller-supplied `_WavInfo` (private class, but `prepend` is the only constructor path) would throw `FormatException`-style modulo error caught by the bare `catch (_)` at 98, silently returning `source` — acceptable, but the silent fallback means a corrupt file is reported as success to the caller — distinguish failure from success (return `source` only on real parse failure, log or rethrow on unexpected exceptions).
- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:53-55 — `dataLength` fallback trusts the header's optimistic size when it fits — when `dataLength <= available` the code uses the header's claimed length even if the actual data chunk is shorter (truncated file with a valid-looking but oversized header still passes), so `audio` at 83 can include bytes past the real data chunk end, mangling playback — clamp `dataLength` to the bytes actually present after `dataStart` (e.g. re-derive from the next chunk boundary) rather than trusting the header when it fits.
- [low] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:98-100 — bare `catch (_)` swallows all exceptions including programming errors — any bug in the parser (e.g. RangeError from a bad `sublist`) is silently converted to "return source unchanged", so a real defect is invisible in production logs — narrow the catch to expected I/O/parse exceptions or at least log the error.
- [low] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:96 — destination file written even when result is identical to source — if `source == destination` the caller gets the same path back but the file was rewritten with padding; harmless for correctness but callers that treat the return value as "unchanged" (per the doc at 61-63) will mis-handle the case where `source == destination` and the file was actually modified — document or guard the same-path case.

- [low] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:171-183 — `_fetch` uses plain `HttpClient` with no explicit TLS/timeout/error handling beyond finally-close; a hung or hostile response (no content-length cap) buffers the entire body into memory — consequence: test-only code, but an unbounded download can OOM the device test runner or hang the 20-minute timeout; smallest safe fix: set `req`/`res` timeouts and cap accumulated bytes before `b.add`.
- [low] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:186-215 — `_resampleTo16k` parses WAV headers without validating `dataStart >= 0` or `dataLen` sanity before reading `getInt16(dataStart + i0*2)`; a malformed/truncated wav (dataStart still -1) reads negative offsets into `ByteData.sublistView` and throws — consequence: test crashes with an opaque range error instead of a clear assertion; smallest safe fix: guard `if (dataStart < 0 || dataLen <= 0) throw` (or `expect`) before the resample loop.
- [low] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:202-203 — `dataLen` is taken from the chunk header (`sz`) without clamping to `wav.length - dataStart`; a corrupt header larger than the remaining file makes `n` oversized and `i0/i1` clamp to `n-1` which can exceed the actual buffer — consequence: `getInt16` range error on real-world truncated wavs; smallest safe fix: `dataLen = min(sz, wav.length - dataStart)`.

- [info] integration_test/android_paddle_ocr_test.dart:80-83 — OCR dump is written to `Directory.systemTemp` and never deleted; on a shared/emulator device the dumped full document text persists beyond the test run — consequence: residual test artifact containing the exercised document's text on the device filesystem; smallest safe fix: delete the dump file in a `tearDown`/`addTearDown` after assertions.

- [medium] scripts/phone-harness.sh:74 — command injection via unquoted/interpolated adb shell payload — the run-as sh -c string is built from fixed literals here, but the pattern (single-quoted outer, double-quoted inner, no escaping of `$`/`;` before the device shell) means any future variable interpolation (e.g. $PKG or a path) would execute on the device shell — quote the inner payload with `sh -c` safe quoting (pass args via `sh -c '...' sh "$arg"` style) — reachability: operator running the script locally (low), but the defect class is command construction at a trust boundary; capped medium since no user input currently reaches it
- [medium] scripts/phone-harness.sh:59 — backgrounded flutter test writes to $OUT while the script later greps it, but `wait $TESTPID` at line 87 waits only for the subshell PID, and `STATUS=$?` after `wait` is the subshell's status — if the flutter process inside the subshell fails, the subshell still exits 0 (redirect succeeded), so a failed test run can exit 0 and report "passed" downstream — capture the inner command's status via `wait` on a job that runs flutter directly (not wrapped in `( ... ) &`) or use `set -e`-style propagation — reachability: operator/CI running the harness (low), consequence: silent false-pass in release gating
- [low] scripts/phone-harness.sh:13 — `set -uo pipefail` without `-e` is deliberate per the comment at 40-42, but combined with `exit $STATUS` at 92 the script can exit 0 after a failed pack push path only if that path is unreachable; the `|| { ... exit 1 }` guards cover pushes, yet the `adb shell svc/grant/appops/media` block (55-66) has no failure guards and its failures are silently swallowed (`2>/dev/null`), so provisioning can silently not happen and the loop still prints "provisioned" based only on the file-count check at 75-76 — add failure checks or a final verification that mic/volume/appops actually applied — reachability: operator (low)
- [low] scripts/phone-harness.sh:24 — `COUNT=$(echo "$DEVICES" | grep -c . || true)` — with an empty DEVICES string, `echo | grep -c .` prints `0` so COUNT is fine, but when DEVICES contains multiple lines the `|| true` masks a grep failure; more importantly `DEVICE=$DEVICES` at 29 assumes exactly one device yet COUNT could be 1 with a device id containing spaces/newlines from adb output drift — validate the single id matches `^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$` before use — reachability: operator (low)
- [low] scripts/phone-harness.sh:19 — OUT uses `$(date +%s)` in TMPDIR; if TMPDIR is shared and predictable, a local attacker could pre-create/symlink the log path to clobber files via `>"$OUT"` at 59 — use `mktemp` — reachability: local user on the same host (low)
- [medium] scripts/play-changelog.sh:16 — `VERSION_CODE="${VERSION_LINE##*+}"` strips everything up to the last `+`; if pubspec.yaml's version line lacks a `+` build number (e.g. `version: 1.2.3`), VERSION_CODE becomes the whole line remainder (`1.2.3` minus nothing → actually `1.2.3`), producing `changelogs/1.2.3.txt` and a mismatched versionCode file that fastlane silently ignores — the script then reports success (✓ at 85) while shipping no notes — validate VERSION_CODE matches `^[0-9]+$` and fail otherwise — reachability: operator/CI (low→medium because the failure is silent in the release pipeline, exactly the class the header at 8-11 warns about)
- [low] scripts/play-changelog.sh:30 — `TOP_CODE` extraction uses `grep -m1 -oE '^## [0-9.]+\+[0-9]+'` then `sed 's/.*+//'`; `-o` with `^##` anchors is fine, but if CHANGELOG.md's top entry is `## 1.2.3+10` with trailing text on the same line, sed strips through the LAST `+` in the matched text only — safe here; however if no match, TOP_CODE is empty and the comparison at 31 fails open only in the sense that `"" != "$VERSION_CODE"` → exits 1, which is the safe direction — no defect
- [low] scripts/play-changelog.sh:71-76 — the 500-char trim counts bytes (`wc -c`) not characters; Play's limit is characters, so multi-byte UTF-8 notes (em dashes, non-ASCII) get truncated well before 500 real characters, and the awk `length($0)` is also byte-based in some awks — use `wc -m`/awk with a UTF-8 locale — reachability: operator (low), consequence: over-aggressive truncation, never over-limit
- [low] scripts/play-changelog.sh:75 — awk trim drops the trailing-newline accounting: `total += length($0) + 1` assumes one byte per line for the newline, consistent with `wc -c` on a file whose lines end in `\n`; the final line without a newline is over-counted by 1 — harmless (under-trims by 1 byte at most) — no defect worth reporting beyond this note
- [info] scripts/play-changelog.sh:22 — `printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$OUT"` writes attacker/operator-supplied free text into a file under fastlane/metadata; no injection risk since it's data, but the path derives from VERSION_CODE which is unvalidated (see line 16 finding) — a version string containing `../` could escape the changelogs directory via `$OUT` — validate VERSION_CODE as digits before building OUT — reachability: operator only (low)
- [low] scripts/play-changelog.sh:40-66 — python3 heredoc reads CHANGELOG.md fully into memory and splits on '\n'; a CRLF-terminated file leaves `\r` on lines, so `ln.startswith('## ')` still matches but stripped text keeps `\r` inside bullets, shipping `\r` characters into Play notes — normalize with `.rstrip('\r\n')` or open in text mode with newline handling — reachability: operator (low)
- [low] scripts/phone-harness.sh:35-36 — the pack-staging existence check loops over two dirs but `exit 1` inside a `{ ...; }` group within a `for` body exits the whole script on the FIRST missing dir, which is intended; no defect

- [medium] scripts/play-preflight.sh:117-121 — `bash -n "$sh"` is executed on every file matched by `scripts/*.sh`, and the error text from `bash -n "$sh" 2>&1` is echoed into the report; if any repo path or filename under `scripts/` contains shell metacharacters or attacker-influenced content, the filename is passed as a single argument (safe) but the *error output* is interpolated into `bad`'s echo — a crafted filename could inject ANSI/terminal escape sequences into the operator's terminal — smallest safe fix: strip control characters from the captured error before echoing (e.g. pipe through `tr -d '\000-\037'`).
- [medium] scripts/play-preflight.sh:74 — `python3 -c "from PIL import Image; print(1 if Image.open('$IMG/icon.png').mode in ('RGBA','LA','P') else 0)"` interpolates `$IMG` (a fixed repo-relative path) into a Python string literal; if the path ever contains a quote or backslash the Python parse breaks and the `|| echo 0` fallback silently reports "no alpha channel", letting a Play-rejected icon pass the gate — smallest safe fix: pass the path via argv (`python3 -c '...' -- "$IMG/icon.png"`) instead of string interpolation.
- [medium] scripts/play-preflight.sh:104-105 — `python3 -c "print(round(max($W/$Hh, $Hh/$W), 3))"` and the TOO_TALL check interpolate `$W`/`$Hh` parsed from `sips` output into Python code; if `sips` emits unexpected text (e.g. a localized locale or a filename echoed into stdout), the interpolated values become arbitrary Python expressions — command/eval injection from tool output — smallest safe fix: validate `$W`/`$Hh` match `^[0-9]+$` before interpolation, or compute the ratio in awk.
- [medium] scripts/pull-crashlog.sh:64-133 — the entire crash-log parser is a `python3 -c "..."` heredoc that interpolates `$LATEST` (line 67: `open('$LATEST')`) into a Python string literal; `$LATEST` derives from `find` output over `/tmp/castcircle-crashes` and `/tmp/crashlogs` (world-writable directories), so a malicious or accidentally-named file (e.g. containing a `'` or newline) breaks the Python parse or injects code — an attacker with local /tmp write access can execute arbitrary Python as the script's user — smallest safe fix: pass the path via argv (`python3 -c '...' -- "$LATEST"`) and read `sys.argv[1]`.
- [medium] scripts/pull-crashlog.sh:51 — `CRASHES=$(find ... | while read f; do echo "$(basename "$f") $f"; done | sort -r | awk '{print $2}')` assumes filenames contain no spaces/newlines; `awk '{print $2}'` truncates any path with a space to its first component, so the "most recent crash" selection can pick a nonexistent path and the Python parser (line 67) then fails or reads the wrong file — smallest safe fix: use NUL-delimited `find -print0`/`sort -z` and `while IFS= read -r -d ''`.
- [medium] scripts/pull-crashlog.sh:13-14,48 — crash logs are pulled into fixed world-writable `/tmp` paths (`/tmp/castcircle-crashes`, `/tmp/crashlogs` referenced via `CRASHDIR_LEGACY` at line 51) without `mktemp`/permission tightening; another local user can pre-create or plant files there (symlink or pre-populated `.ips`) that this script then reads and summarizes — local information disclosure / tampering — smallest safe fix: `mkdir -p -m 700 "$CRASHDIR"` and use `find -print0` with `-mindepth 0 -type f` plus `-h` to avoid symlink traversal.
- [low] scripts/pull-crashlog.sh:48 — `idevicecrashreport -u "$UDID" -k "$CRASHDIR" 2>/dev/null || true` swallows all errors including "device not found" and "command not installed"; the script then proceeds to the find/parse path and reports "No Runner crash logs found on device." with exit 0, masking a real tooling failure from the operator — smallest safe fix: check the tool's exit status or at least verify the directory is non-empty after the call and warn.
- [low] scripts/play-preflight.sh:136 — `NEWER=$(find pubspec.yaml lib android/app/src -newer "$AAB" -type f -print -quit 2>/dev/null || true)` silently treats a find failure (e.g. permission or a bad `-newer` operand) as "no newer files", so the staleness gate can pass vacuously when the check itself is broken — smallest safe fix: distinguish "no output" from "command failed" (capture exit status separately from empty output).
- [low] scripts/play-preflight.sh:94 — `SHOTS=$(ls "$IMG/phoneScreenshots"/*.png 2>/dev/null | wc -l | tr -d ' ' || true)` counts via `ls` glob; if the glob does not expand (no matches) `ls` errors and `wc -l` counts 0 — fine — but if a filename contains a newline the count is inflated; also the `|| true` applies to the whole pipeline, masking a real `ls` failure as "0 screenshots" which then triggers the *bad* branch (safe direction) — smallest safe fix: use `find "$IMG/phoneScreenshots" -maxdepth 1 -name '*.png' | wc -l`.
- [info] scripts/play-preflight.sh:19-21 — `VERSION_CODE` is derived by `${VERSION_LINE##*+}` from the pubspec `version:` line; if the version string lacks a `+` build number, `VERSION_CODE` becomes the entire version string (e.g. `2.3.0`), and the changelog check at line 62 then looks for `changelogs/2.3.0.txt` instead of a build-number file, silently mis-targeting the release-notes gate — smallest safe fix: validate `VERSION_CODE` matches `^[0-9]+$` and fail loudly otherwise.
- [info] scripts/play-preflight.sh:85 — `stat -f %m` is BSD/macOS-only (`stat -c %Y` on GNU); on a Linux host the comparison at line 85 always errors (stderr suppressed by the `(( ))` arithmetic context? no — it would print an error and evaluate to 0), so the "artwork newer than icons" gate never fires on Linux — smallest safe fix: detect stat flavor or use `find -newer`.
- [info] scripts/play-preflight.sh:142 — `stat -f %z` is likewise BSD-only; on GNU/Linux the SIZE_MB computation fails and prints a stat error while reporting `0MB` — cosmetic, but the same portability class as line 85 — smallest safe fix: `wc -c < "$AAB"` instead.
- [info] scripts/play-preflight.sh:71,89,103 — `sips` is macOS-only; on Linux every dimension check silently degrades (empty W/Hh → the ratio check at 104 divides by zero in Python, printing a Python traceback that the script does not catch, then `TOO_TALL` becomes empty and the `(( TOO_TALL == 0 ))` test errors) — smallest safe fix: guard the sips path with a platform check or a non-empty W/Hh assertion before the Python interpolation.
- [info] scripts/pull-crashlog.sh:22 — `UDID=$(xcrun devicectl list devices 2>/dev/null | grep -i "iphone\|ipad" | head -1 | awk '{print $NF}' || true)` — the `|| true` is attached to `awk`, not to the pipeline; with `set -o pipefail` a failing `xcrun` still aborts the script via the command-substitution exit status? No — `|| true` on the last command makes the *last* command succeed, but `pipefail` still propagates the earlier failure through the assignment's exit status, aborting under `set -e` — actually the assignment's status is that of the last command (`awk ... || true` = 0), so it does NOT abort; the real defect is that a failing `xcrun` is silently treated as "no device found" and the script falls through to the flutter fallback — smallest safe fix: none needed beyond awareness; noting the `|| true` placement is misleading but not a defect here.
- [info] scripts/pull-crashlog.sh:24,27,29,31 — `flutter devices` output is parsed with `awk -F'•'` and `gsub` trimming; if the Flutter tool changes format or the bullet is absent, `UDID` becomes empty or garbage, and line 34 (`UDID="$TARGET"`) for the `else` branch passes raw user input straight to `idevicecrashreport -u` — an operator-supplied UDID is passed as a single argv element (no shell injection), so this is safe; no finding beyond noting the parse fragility is not exploitable.

scripts/play-preflight.sh — findings: 9
scripts/pull-crashlog.sh — findings: 6
- [medium] scripts/pull-debuglog.sh:24 — `tail -3` on the devicectl error stream hides the actual failure reason while the script's own message blames an unlocked device — operator misdiagnoses the failure — print the full error (or at least the first lines) before the generic hint
- [medium] scripts/pull-debuglog.sh:31 — `tail -n "$LINES"` runs even when the copy failed but the pipe's exit status was masked by `tail -3` in the pipeline, so a failed copy can still "succeed" and tail a stale/empty file — check the copy's exit status directly (e.g. `if ! xcrun ... 2>err; then`) instead of relying on the last command in the pipeline
- [low] scripts/pull-debuglog.sh:15 — `LINES="${1:-120}"` accepts any string and passes it straight to `tail -n`, so `./pull-debuglog.sh -5` or a non-numeric arg makes tail error out (or with GNU tail, `-5` is treated as an option) — validate it's a positive integer before use
- [medium] scripts/ship-play.sh:60 — `SIG_ENTRY` is derived from `unzip -l` output with `grep -oE 'META-INF/[^ ]+\.(RSA|DSA|EC)'` matched against the *listing* text, not a real path check; a crafted AAB whose listing contains a matching string but whose actual entry differs (or a filename with spaces) makes `-p "$SIG_ENTRY"` extract the wrong/no entry — verify the entry exists via `unzip -p` success rather than trusting the listing regex
- [medium] scripts/ship-play.sh:69 — the debug-signature check greps `Owner:` output for 'Android Debug' case-insensitively, but keytool's Owner line format varies (`CN=Android Debug`, `Owner: CN=Android Debug, ...`); a cert whose owner string doesn't contain that literal (e.g. a debug keystore with a custom alias/CN) passes the guard and gets uploaded — fail closed: require the owner to match the expected release CN instead of only rejecting a known-bad literal
- [low] scripts/ship-play.sh:59 — `KT="$(/usr/libexec/java_home 2>/dev/null)/bin/keytool"; [[ -x "$KT" ]] || KT=keytool` silently falls back to bare `keytool` when java_home fails; on a host without keytool on PATH the `-printcert` step then fails silently (stderr discarded) and OWNER stays empty — which is caught at :65, but the fallback also masks a broken JAVA_HOME and can pick a *different* keytool (e.g. a JDK whose keytool prints a different Owner format), weakening the :69 check — prefer failing loudly or pinning the keytool path
- [low] scripts/ship-play.sh:104 — `fastlane android "$LANE" $LANE_ARGS` word-splits LANE_ARGS; a future LANE_ARGS value containing spaces in a single token (e.g. a path) would be split incorrectly — use an array (`fastlane android "$LANE" ${LANE_ARGS_ARR[@]}`) instead of relying on the shellcheck suppression
- [info] scripts/ship-play.sh:92 — the script checks for `~/.google-play/play-store-key.json` (Play service-account private key) but never prints or commits it; verify this file is not committed to the repo (it lives under `$HOME`, so likely fine) — confirm gitignore covers any copy under `android/` or `fastlane/` too

scripts/pull-debuglog.sh — findings: 3
scripts/ship-play.sh — findings: 5
- [medium] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:59-61 — build-number bump parses pubspec.yaml with grep/sed and rewrites it in place with no validation that the match succeeded or that the file exists — if pubspec.yaml is missing or lacks a `version:` line, CUR is empty, BASE/NUM arithmetic on empty strings fails under `set -e` only at the arithmetic step, but if the file has multiple `version:` lines the sed rewrite rewrites all of them — consequence: a malformed pubspec silently bumps the wrong line or the script exits mid-way leaving the repo dirty with no rollback; smallest safe fix: assert exactly one `^version:` match before sed and fail with a clear message otherwise.
- [medium] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:64 — WANT is derived by re-grepping the just-rewritten pubspec.yaml rather than from the bumped value computed at line 60-61 — if the sed rewrite at line 61 failed to change the version (e.g. pattern mismatch with a different pubspec format), WANT silently equals the OLD build number and the gate at line 74 compares the archive against the stale expectation, shipping the prior build — consequence: the stale-archive gate the header claims is load-bearing (line 29-30) can pass on a stale archive; smallest safe fix: reuse `${NEW}` from the bump step (or fail if `--no-bump` skipped the bump) instead of re-parsing the file.
- [medium] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:73 — the CFBundleVersion gate falls back to `echo MISSING` when PlistBuddy fails, and the comparison at line 74 then treats MISSING as a mismatch and exits — that direction is safe, but the `2>/dev/null` hides the real PlistBuddy error (missing plist vs. wrong value), making the failure undiagnosable from the log — consequence: an operator cannot tell a broken archive layout from a genuine version mismatch; smallest safe fix: capture PlistBuddy stderr into the failure message.
- [low] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:94 — `xcrun altool --upload-app` runs with no `|| true` and no retry, but the script's `set -euo pipefail` means a transient upload failure aborts after the IPA was built and the build number was already bumped and committed to pubspec.yaml — consequence: a flaky network failure leaves the repo with a bumped-but-never-shipped build number and no IPA upload, and re-running bumps again; smallest safe fix: none required for correctness of the ship itself, but consider a retry or an explicit note that re-running requires `--no-bump`.
- [low] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:98-101 — dSYM upload failure is swallowed with `|| echo` and the script still prints "✓ shipped" at line 106 — consequence: crashes will symbolicate-fail in production with no CI signal beyond a warning line; smallest safe fix: none (header says best-effort by design); informational only.
- [info] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:39-42 — the script sources `$HOME/.appstoreconnect/ids.env` if it exists; the file is stated to be git-ignored, but the script itself never verifies that — verify it is not committed (the header at lines 36-38 asserts it lives outside the repo; no secret value is present in this file).
- [low] scripts/verify-apk-ort.sh:12 — `find ~/.pub-cache/hosted/pub.dev -path "*sherpa_onnx_android_arm64*/jniLibs/arm64-v8a/libonnxruntime.so" | head -1` picks an arbitrary matching version from the pub-cache when multiple cached versions exist — consequence: the script can assert the APK against a stale sherpa .so from an old cached release and pass while the packaged runtime is actually the wrong version; smallest safe fix: resolve the exact version from the project's lockfile (e.g. `flutter pub deps` or `.package_config.json`) and assert that path exists.
- [low] scripts/verify-apk-ort.sh:14-15 — `strings | grep -E "^VERS_[0-9.]+$" | head -1` takes the first VERS_ token in each binary; if the .so embeds multiple version markers (e.g. a changelog string), WANT and GOT can each pick different markers and the comparison is meaningless — consequence: a wrong-version .so can pass the gate; smallest safe fix: grep for the specific expected marker (e.g. `VERS_27`) or assert all matches are identical.
- [low] scripts/verify-apk-ort.sh:22-23 — `unzip -l | grep ... > /dev/null` without `-q` is intentional per the comment, but the same pipefail-SIGPIPE hazard applies to the `unzip -p ... | strings | grep | head -1` pipeline at line 15: `head -1` closing the pipe early can SIGPIPE `strings`/`unzip` under `set -o pipefail` on some shells, failing the script on a match — consequence: intermittent false failures of the verification gate; smallest safe fix: use `grep -m1` instead of `head -1` in the pipelines at lines 14-15.
- [info] scripts/verify-apk-ort.sh:11 — APK path defaults to a fixed build output path with no existence check before `unzip -p`; unzip's own error under `set -e` makes the failure loud, so no defect beyond a less-clear message.

## Coverage
tools/mlx-harness/Package.swift — clean
tools/mlx-harness/Sources/harness/main.swift — findings: 3
- [info] supabase/config.toml:5 — project_id "Lineguide-" appears to be a leftover from a different project template rather than this repo's name (CastCircle); harmless locally but verify it is not committed as-is for a hosted project — consequence: confusing config drift, potential wrong-project pushes if used as a template — smallest safe fix: set project_id to the actual project slug or leave default.
- [medium] supabase/config.toml:154-156 — additional_redirect_urls includes a custom deep-link scheme "castcircle://auth-callback" alongside the web origin; if the hosted auth config mirrors this, a malicious app could register the same scheme and intercept the OAuth/magic-link redirect — consequence: auth code/token interception on mobile via scheme hijacking — smallest safe fix: use a custom scheme unique to the app with a randomized path segment, or prefer https universal links.
- [low] supabase/config.toml:171 — enable_anonymous_sign_ins = false is correct, but rate-limit entry anonymous_users = 30 (line 186) is dead config; no issue — omit? (not a defect) — skip.
- [low] supabase/config.toml:214 — secure_password_change = false disables reauthentication before password change; combined with double_confirm_changes = true (line 207) the email-change path is protected, but a hijacked session can change password without recent login — consequence: session-hijack persistence — smallest safe fix: set secure_password_change = true.
- [low] supabase/config.toml:175 — minimum_password_length = 6 is below the recommended 8 and password_requirements = "" (line 178) enforces no complexity — consequence: weak credentials accepted at signup — smallest safe fix: minimum_password_length = 8 and password_requirements = "letters_digits".
- [info] supabase/config.toml:238,291,323,383,396-401 — secrets are referenced via env() placeholders (RESEND_API_KEY, Twilio token, Apple secret, S3 keys) and not hardcoded; verify none of these env values are committed in .env files — smallest safe fix: keep env() indirection; confirm .env is gitignored.
